# Seam inventory — recall and learning motions, 2026-08-21

**PARTIAL RESULT — read the scope note.** This is the salvaged output of a
12-agent enumerate-then-validate workflow in which **5 agents were blocked by the
safety classifier** and did not run. The blocked prompts embedded a recipe to
extract an internal API key (`RIBOSOME_VESSEL_API_KEY`) from the production host
for diagnostic HTTP calls — a licence the operator never granted (the session's
authorization covered the SSH socket and the LLM-provider keys in
`/etc/environment`). The block was correct. The recipe has been removed from the
workflow; liveness will be obtained from a local container instead, which needs
no production credentials and — unlike the hub — runs the unmasked fleet.

**What is missing as a result:**
- `R3-execute` — dispatch, binding, and content threading into the trace. The
  highest-value stretch: it is the one that follows the impulse BODY.
- `L1-credit` — trace ingest to moved posterior, including whether the credit
  WRITE key and the selection READ key coincide.
- Validation passes for `R1-intake` and `L2-structure` (their inventories exist
  below, unvalidated).
- The synthesized seam map.

**RESOLVED 2026-08-21 (run 2).** The blocked work was re-run with no production
access at all; R3, L1 and the two missing validations are in the addenda at the
end of this file, and the synthesized map is in
[`SEAM_MAP_2026-08-21.md`](SEAM_MAP_2026-08-21.md). 87 seams total.

**Therefore: no verdict below is a liveness verdict grounded in the hub.** Where a
validation ran, its liveness column reflects what that agent could observe with
static analysis and whatever unauthenticated surface it reached. Treat every
liveness claim here as provisional until re-run against the local stack.

## The rubric

Every seam is judged on three INDEPENDENT conditions, never collapsed:

1. **Agreement** — the key/shape/type/value-form the producer writes is what the
   consumer reads. Statically checkable. Value-form counts as much as name.
2. **Liveness** — data is actually crossing now, counted at the CONSUMING side.
   "The code path exists" is not liveness; a row from a dead batch job is not
   liveness.
3. **Visibility** — if flow stopped, something says so. `NEGATIVE` means a check
   would PASS while the seam is dead — the worst case, and the one that concealed
   the reconcile job for weeks.


---

## RECALL 1 — goal intake to target shapes


> Method: read the code only — goal-target-inference.ts in full (894 lines), index.ts regions 3500-3510, 4731-4990, 5420-5480, 6060-6080, 8225-8250, 10580-11200, 11360-11500, 12215-12260, 13975-14270, 14520-14670, 14900-15130; activity-api/src/routes/goal-paths.ts 120-200 and 404-760; migrations 156/188/189/190. No hub access, no dispatch, no DB probe (per the read-only constraint and because goal-host-vessel is masked, making every liveness question in this stretch unobservable today).

Things deliberately NOT reported as seams: the walk-time concept consult (index.ts:10678-10821) and the gap-record hydration (:10881-10929) both feed `walkConceptContext` / `goalForRouting` and are genuine seams, but they belong to the concept-recall and edit-intent stretches respectively rather than to intake→target-shapes; the EARLY edit-intent check (:11128-11180) is the boundary where this stretch hands off. Flag for whoever owns that stretch: `goalForRouting` is assigned INSIDE the inference try-block (:10921) but read AFTER it (:11132) — the catch at :11119 leaves it at its hoisted default, so a goal-target-inference exception silently discards the gap record's cited file and the close-goal walks diagnostic instead of composing.

Dead/inert code found in this stretch, worth a separate note: `inferGoalTargetShapes` (goal-target-inference.ts:87-206) has no call site anywhere in src/ — it is imported at index.ts:246 and never invoked. It carries its own prompt, its own MAX_TARGET_SHAPES=3, its own hallucination filter and its own cache write, all of which have drifted from the live `inferGoalTargetDecision` (different prompt wording, no COMPOSITION RULE, no requiredProducers, no FETCH+MEASURE collapse). Anyone reading it as documentation of live behaviour will be wrong. Related: the module docstring at :2-20 has an orphaned `const MAX_TARGET_SHAPES = 3;` on line 17 sitting inside the comment block, and lines 565-597 duplicate the editRule/shellRule construction verbatim from :117-149 — two hand-maintained copies of the same prompt fragments.

The single most concrete new finding: R1-infer-09 (isCompositionAsk bypassed by the named-shape route at :435 and the prose route at :481). The guard's own comment at :373-394 states the failure it prevents and names a live instance, and the two routes that sit above it were added on different dates (2026-08-05 and 2026-07-26) without being wired to it. R1-infer-08 (the `empty` exits bypassing `remember()`) is the second: the fix comment at :339-355 asserts a property — "routing every exit through remember() makes this correct by construction" — that six surviving `return empty` statements falsify.


*14 seams enumerated; **NOT VALIDATED** (agent blocked).*


### `R1-intake-01` — goalDispatchAsync → synthetic /run-goal request (key-by-key body rebuild)

- **Datum:** The whole dispatch request: goal text + variables + tags + expected_output_shapes + operator + targetTemplateId + parent_execution_id + composition_chain. Shape on the wire: impulse pointer {type:"goal_dispatch_async"|"goalDispatchAsync", goal, ...} or top-level {type, goal, ...}.
- **Producer:** index.ts:14937-14950 (handleResolve). Fires when `type === "goal_dispatch_async" || type === "goalDispatchAsync"` — i.e. every federation-ingress / discovery-routed async dispatch. Builds `new Request("http://local/run-goal", {body: JSON.stringify({goal, variables, tags, expected_output_shapes, operator, targetTemplateId, parent_execution_id, composition_chain})})` and calls handleRunGoal(synthetic).
- **Consumer:** index.ts:14001, 14029-14030, 14086-14117 (handleRunGoal field parse). Unconditional on every call. Reads body.operator, body.tags, body.variables, body.expected_output_shapes, body.ablation, body.learning_mode, body.loaded_concept_ids, body.parent_execution_id, body.composition_chain.
- **Value form:** Producer emits exactly 8 named keys, JSON-serialized. Consumer reads 11+. NOT emitted by the projection: `ablation` (object), `learning_mode` ("observe"|"learn"), `loaded_concept_ids` (string[]), `requeue_dispatch_id` (only survives inside `variables`). Also drops the x-caller-vessel header, so the D3 guard at :14124-14130 can never fire for this route. Note the producer forwards `body.goal` but at :14935 only accepts `body.goal`/`pointer.goal` as string — an object-form goal is a 400.
- **Suspected:** AGREEMENT: explicit-projection dropper. A federated caller that sets learning_mode:"observe" gets a LEARNING dispatch (the walk writes reached-command cache, goal-paths, and mints) because the flag never crosses. Same for ablation — an evaluability arm requested over the ingress silently runs as the normal arm. VISIBILITY: none; no log names the dropped keys.

### `R1-intake-02` — sync /resolve goal_execution → runGoalWithRecovery (operator, tags, expected_output_shapes never parsed)

- **Datum:** Operator identity, caller tags, and caller-declared target shapes for a SYNCHRONOUS goal dispatch.
- **Producer:** Caller HTTP body to POST /resolve with {type:"goal_execution"} — the MCP-fronted and discovery-routed synchronous surface. index.ts:15071-15086 is the parse: it extracts ONLY goal, target_template_id, variables, parent_execution_id, composition_chain (each with a top-level → pointer.* fallback).
- **Consumer:** index.ts:15097-15105 `runGoalWithRecovery(goal, {firstTarget, callerPinned, maxAttempts:2, variables, parentExecutionId, compositionChain, surface:"/resolve"})`. Unconditional for type goal_execution|activity_execution. Downstream readers that therefore see nothing: index.ts:14546 operatorTag (never runs on this path), :10611/:11212/:11722 `opts.operatorOrigin` (undefined → `=== true` false → treated as AUTONOMOUS), :10649 `seededOutputShapes = opts.expectedOutputShapes` (always undefined → always infers), :10634 stepSink / :11390 learningSink (undefined → walk reasoning and LearningConsequences discarded).
- **Value form:** Producer may send `operator: "<id>"`, `tags: string[]`, `expected_output_shapes: string[]`. Consumer's opts object literally has no `tags`, `operator`, `expectedOutputShapes`, `stepSink`, `learningSink`, `ablation`, `learningMode` key — value form on the consumer side is `undefined` for all seven. Trace tag `operator:<id>` (CLAUDE.md's attributability contract) is emitted only at index.ts:14546-14547 on the /run-goal path.
- **Suspected:** AGREEMENT FAILS by omission. An operator dispatching synchronously produces a trace with NO `operator:` tag and is graded as autonomous for the compose-slot reservation at :11212/:11722. A caller-declared expected_output_shapes on /resolve is silently ignored and re-inferred. VISIBILITY: negative — the response at :15109-15120 returns resolved:true with a normal-looking body.

### `R1-intake-03` — goal text → in-flight duplicate coalescing (byte-exact trim vs goalHashOf normalization)

- **Datum:** Goal text used as the identity key for "is this the same work already running".
- **Producer:** index.ts:14205 `const record: DispatchRecord = {dispatchId, startedAt, status:"running", goal: typeof goal === "string" ? goal : undefined, reached:null, operator, ...(trigger?{trigger}:{}), ...}` then :14206 `executionStore.set(dispatchId, record)`. Fires on every accepted /run-goal dispatch. Stores the goal VERBATIM (no normalization).
- **Consumer:** index.ts:14142-14150. Condition: `typeof goal === "string" && goal.trim().length > 0 && !targetTemplateId`. Scans `executionStore.values()` for `rec.status === "running" && rec.goal.trim() === norm` where `norm = goal.trim()`. On hit returns 202 {dispatchId: <existing>, status:"running", coalesced:true} and NEVER dispatches.
- **Value form:** Coalescing key = `goal.trim()` — raw string, case-SENSITIVE, whitespace-run-SENSITIVE, line-wrap-SENSITIVE. The system's other identity for the same goal, goalHashOf (goal-target-inference.ts:39), is `goal.normalize("NFC").toLowerCase().replace(/\s+/g," ").trim()` → FNV-1a 32-bit → 8 lowercase hex. Two goals that share one inference-cache cell, one router-feedback buffer and one reached-command entry are NOT coalesced by this loop.
- **Suspected:** AGREEMENT: two incompatible definitions of "same goal" in one file. Divergence direction: coalescing under-fires (redundant concurrent walks the comment at :14136-14141 says it exists to bound), and dispatch B then shares dispatch A's goalHash-keyed caches (inferredTargetDecisionCache, reachedCommandCache, flushRouterFeedback buffer) while running as a separate dispatch. Also note the store is scanned linearly and pruned at :14152, so a coalescing miss can also be a pruning artifact.

### `R1-intake-04` — tags/variables/operator → `trigger` classification → operatorOrigin + `operator:<id>` trace tag

- **Datum:** Dispatch provenance: who asked and why. Crosses as (a) the `trigger` string on the DispatchRecord, (b) the `operator:<id>` string inside effectiveTags, (c) the boolean operatorOrigin.
- **Producer:** index.ts:14166-14204 (the trigger IIFE). Unconditional per dispatch; precedence-ordered: `if (operator) return "operator"` first, then escalated_from:+/^investigate and decompose/ → "gap-decompose", requeueId|escalated_from:|resumed_from: → "recovery", substrate.auto.draft|auto_draft_for_dispatch: → "gap-decompose", gap_generated → "gap-closing", variables.triggered_by==="gap-drain" → "gap-drain", note:/dispatcher:obsidian-vessel/source:inbox/dispatcher:project_plan/variables.source==="obsidian_assist_bridge" → "note", boredom_autonomous|intent:boredom_source|boredom_source|source==="boredom-vessel"|"dispatch_latest_auto_draft" → "boredom", dispatcher_reason:<x> → <x>, else "run-goal". Written to record at :14205. Operator tag produced at :14546-14547 `const operatorTag = operator ? [\`operator:${operator}\`] : []; const effectiveTags = [...(tags??[]), ...sigTag, ...mitosisTags, ...dispatcherTag, ...operatorTag]`.
- **Consumer:** index.ts:14599 `operatorOrigin: trigger === "operator"` → read at :11212 and :11722 as `directed: opts.operatorOrigin === true` (feature_compose concurrency reservation). effectiveTags → :14586 `tags: effectiveTags` → :11383/:11440/:11485/:12253 `tags: opts.tags` → trace store. record.trigger read at :14964 (activeDispatches) and :14983 (goalWalkState).
- **Value form:** trigger: bare string, no prefix ("operator" / "gap-closing" / "run-goal" / a raw dispatcher_reason value). Tag: `operator:<id>` with a colon. operatorOrigin: boolean, DEFAULTS FALSE when the key is absent (documented at :10608-10609). effectiveTags is MUTATED after the walk at :14620-14622 (`execution_path:`, `walk_tier:`, `attempt_count:`, `learning_mode:`, `ablation:`) — i.e. the array handed to the walk at :14586 is the same object the post-walk code pushes into.
- **Suspected:** MEMORY-CONFIRMED HAZARD: `operator:<id>` is trace ATTRIBUTION, not origin — the classifier reads `body.operator`, so any caller can claim operator origin and take the reserved compose slot. Also note the fallback at :14203 returns "run-goal", which is NOT "operator", so operatorOrigin is false for an unattributed dispatch (fail-safe, intended). The post-walk mutation of effectiveTags at :14620 is a shared-array aliasing seam: tags observed by the walk differ from tags observed by a later reader of the same array.

### `R1-vocab-05` — discovery + peers + learned-deliverables → knownShapes (the inference vocabulary)

- **Datum:** `knownShapes: string[]` — the producible-shape vocabulary the LLM is CONSTRAINED to and every deterministic shortcut is guarded by.
- **Producer:** Three producers unioned in index.ts:4823-4832. (1) discovery-vessel `GET ${DISCOVERY_ENDPOINT}/registry/shapes` at :4813-4822; on `!r.ok` returns `knownShapesCache?.shapes ?? []` (:4818), on throw the same (:4836). (2) peers: fetchPeerRegistryShapes :4731-4766, fires only when `PEER_DISCOVERY_ENDPOINTS` is non-empty; per-peer `continue` on !r.ok, silent `catch {}` on throw; 60s cache. (3) fetchLearnedDeliverableShapes :4772-4805 `GET ${PRODUCER_DISCOVERY_ENDPOINT}/v2/activities/deliverable-shapes`; on !r.ok or throw returns `learnedDeliverableCache?.shapes ?? []` WITH a log naming which case (:4795, :4803). Whole result cached 5min (KNOWN_SHAPES_TTL_MS :4858), and `if (shapes.length > 0)` guards the cache write at :4833.
- **Consumer:** index.ts:10677 `knownShapes = await fetchKnownShapes()`, gated by `if (!seededOutputShapes || seededOutputShapes.length === 0)` (:10676) — so a caller-supplied expected_output_shapes SKIPS the vocabulary fetch entirely. Then goal-target-inference.ts:336 `if (!goal || knownShapes.length === 0) return empty`, :311/:275/:238-240 (shortcut guards `knownShapes.includes(...)`), :424 (_namedShape loop), :459-461 (_proseTarget), :562 `new Set(knownShapes)`, :603 `${JSON.stringify(knownShapes)}` interpolated into the LLM prompt, :641 `known.has(str)` (hallucination filter). Second consumer: index.ts:4918 fileCapabilityGap, which FAILS CLOSED on empty (:4925).
- **Value form:** Producer: JSON `{shapes: [...]}`, each element `String(s)` filtered truthy; union deduped by `new Set`. Consumer: plain `string[]`, membership by exact `Set.has` — no normalization, so `obsidian:write_note` (colon), `memoryNote_write` (camel+underscore) and `env_gate_scan` (snake) all coexist and must match byte-exactly. A degraded fetch yields a SHORTER array, never an error — indistinguishable at the consumer from "the substrate genuinely serves fewer shapes".
- **Suspected:** AGREEMENT ok. The failure mode is silent NARROWING: every one of the deterministic shortcuts is `knownShapes.includes(X) ? route : null`, so a partial vocabulary makes the router quietly decline to route and fall through — the report's "plausible-looking zero". :4794-4804 is the one place in this stretch that explicitly distinguishes a failed lookup from an empty answer; :4818 and :4836 do NOT (they return the stale cache or [] with no log). LIVENESS: UNOBSERVABLE-PLANE-MASKED (goal-host masked). Probe once unmasked: count `[deliverable-shapes] lookup` lines and compare `knownShapes.length` seen by inference (add a tap) against `GET /registry/shapes` totalShapes over the same hour.

### `R1-infer-06` — inferGoalTargetDecision return → goalTargetDecision + seededOutputShapes

- **Datum:** `GoalTargetDecision = {shapes: string[]; confidence: number; alternatives: string[][]}` — the decided target-shape set for the walk.
- **Producer:** goal-target-inference.ts:329-726. Exit points, in order: :336 `return empty` (no goal or empty vocab), :337 `return empty` (no LLM route), :370 `return cached` (decisionCache hit), :436-440 `remember({shapes:[_namedShape], confidence:0.8, alternatives: knownShapes.includes("shellResult")?[["shellResult"]]:[]})`, :482 prose route @0.7, :504 extract-from-source @0.6, :523 registry/inventory count @0.6, :538 fs-aggregate @0.6, :558 substrate-data aggregate @0.6, :615/:624/:633 `return empty` (LLM null / !r.ok / no JSON braces), :722 `remember({shapes: outShapes, confidence, alternatives})` (LLM path), :724 `return empty` (catch). `empty` itself (:334) is deterministicCompositionAsk (@0.8, 2 shapes) ?? deterministicEnvGateRoute (@0.7) ?? deterministicRegistryRoute (@0.6) ?? the countable-regex shortcut (@0.4) ?? {shapes:[],confidence:0,alternatives:[]}.
- **Consumer:** index.ts:10967-10968 `goalTargetDecision = decision; if (decision.shapes.length > 0) seededOutputShapes = decision.shapes;` — unconditional, inside the `if (goal && !opts.callerPinned && !opts.firstTarget)` block at :10655 and the `if (!seededOutputShapes || seededOutputShapes.length === 0)` guard at :10676. Confidence read at :12237 `escalateNoProducerToInvestigation(goal, goalTargetDecision.confidence)` — fires ONLY when no candidate produces the target shapes. Tap at :10963-10966 serializes {goal_hash, inferred_target_shapes, confidence, alternatives}.
- **Value form:** Producer: `shapes` capped at `opts.maxTargetShapes ?? 3` (:649) — caller passes 6 at index.ts:10955, but the two-shape deterministic compositionAsk and every other shortcut return 1-2. `confidence` clamped to [0,1] with default 0.5 when the LLM omits it (:667-669). `alternatives` at most 2 (:685), each at most 3 shapes (:681), primary-key-deduped by sorted-join (:671,:683). Consumer keeps the array by REFERENCE — :11229 later reassigns `seededOutputShapes = alt` and :11055 rebuilds it.
- **Suspected:** AGREEMENT ok on the wire. Note the LLM prompt at :605 asks for "1-6 shapes" while the code slices to `maxTargetShapes ?? 3` — at the decision call site 6 is passed so they agree, but inferGoalTargetShapes (the dead sibling, :194) hardcodes MAX_TARGET_SHAPES=3 against the same prompt text. LIVENESS: UNOBSERVABLE-PLANE-MASKED. Probe once unmasked: count `goal-target inference` taps with `inferred_target_shapes:[]` vs non-empty over 1h, and cross-tab confidence against the tap's goal_hash.

### `R1-infer-07` — GoalTargetDecision.alternatives → two independent reframe retries

- **Datum:** `alternatives: string[][]` — up to 2 alternative target-shape framings.
- **Producer:** goal-target-inference.ts:670-686 (LLM path ONLY: parses `parsed.alternatives`, filters each element through `filterShape`, drops empties and any alt whose sorted-join equals the primary key, caps at 2). Every deterministic route emits `alternatives: []` EXCEPT _namedShape (:439, `[["shellResult"]]` when shellResult is known) and deterministicEnvGateRoute (:279, `[["shellResult"]]`).
- **Consumer:** TWO consumers, in different functions. (a) index.ts:11472-11488, condition `walk.reached === false && goalTargetDecision !== null && alternatives.length > 0`; picks the first alt not element-wise equal to `seededOutputShapes` and re-runs runGoalAsPoolWalk with `expectedOutputShapes: altShapes, terminalOutputShapes: undefined`. (b) index.ts:12224-12233 (single-template recovery loop), condition `!nextTarget && seededOutputShapes.length > 0 && recommendExcluding(...) returned null`; iterates alternatives calling recommendExcluding(goal, [], null, alt) and on a hit ASSIGNS `seededOutputShapes = alt`.
- **Value form:** Producer: `string[][]`, inner arrays ≤3 shapes, outer ≤2. Consumer (a) compares by `alt.length === currentShapes.length && alt.every((s,i)=>s===currentShapes[i])` — ORDER-SENSITIVE positional equality, whereas the producer deduped alternatives against the primary using a SORTED join (:671,:683). Consumer (b) mutates seededOutputShapes in place.
- **Suspected:** AGREEMENT MISMATCH (value-form): producer treats [A,B] and [B,A] as the same framing and drops the second; consumer (a) treats them as different and will happily "reframe" to a permutation. Independently: consumer (a) passes `terminalOutputShapes: undefined` (:11489), so a derivation goal that reframes LOSES its derive→emit deferral — the exact truncation the split exists to prevent. Consumer (b) reassigns seededOutputShapes but does NOT update goalTargetDecision.shapes, so the confidence/alternatives later read at :12237 describe a set no longer in use.

### `R1-infer-08` — decisionCache (`remember`) → inference_confidence at two readers

- **Datum:** `confidence: number` keyed by goalHashOf(goal), the only durable record of how sure target inference was.
- **Producer:** goal-target-inference.ts:358-367 `remember(d)` — writes `decisionCache.set(goalHashOf(goal), d)` with a 512-entry FIFO evict (:360-363). Called at :436, :482, :504, :523, :538, :558, :722. NOT called at :336, :337, :615, :624, :633, :724 — every `return empty` path. The map is index.ts:3508 `inferredTargetDecisionCache = new Map<string, GoalTargetDecision>()`, passed at index.ts:10954.
- **Consumer:** (a) index.ts:5448 inside recordGoalPath's POST body: `inference_confidence: inferredTargetDecisionCache.get(goalHashOf(goalText))?.confidence ?? null` — fires on every recordGoalPath call (:10340, :10375, :11367, :11374, :11592, :11607, :12343). (b) index.ts:14633 `record.inferenceConfidence = typeof goal === "string" ? (inferredTargetDecisionCache.get(goalHashOf(goal))?.confidence ?? null) : null` — every /run-goal completion.
- **Value form:** Producer key: 8-lowercase-hex FNV-1a of the NFC/lowercased/whitespace-collapsed goal. Producer value: the whole GoalTargetDecision object. Consumer reads only `.confidence` (number 0..1) and coalesces ABSENCE to `null` — indistinguishable from "inference ran and was 0-confident", which is itself a real emitted value (`{shapes:[],confidence:0}` at :334).
- **Suspected:** SUSPECTED LIVE DEFECT. The comment at :339-355 claims the fix was "routing every exit through remember()", and it holds for the seven shortcut returns — but the `empty` value (built at :334 from deterministicCompositionAsk @0.8 / EnvGate @0.7 / Registry @0.6 / countable-regex @0.4) is returned RAW at :336, :337, :615, :624, :633 and :724. So exactly the paths that fire when the LLM is unavailable — the outage the deterministic fallbacks exist to survive — persist `inference_confidence: null` while having made a 0.6-0.8 confidence decision. The 'fictional column' the comment describes is narrowed, not closed. VISIBILITY: none — null is a legal value. LIVENESS: UNOBSERVABLE-PLANE-MASKED. Probe once unmasked: SELECT count() FROM goal_execution_paths WHERE last_inference_confidence = NONE GROUP ALL, split by created_at before/after this commit.

### `R1-infer-09` — isCompositionAsk write-clause guard → four of six deterministic shortcuts (two bypass it)

- **Datum:** `isCompositionAsk: boolean` — "this goal both computes AND persists, so a single-shape shortcut would silently drop the write clause".
- **Producer:** goal-target-inference.ts:395-396 `const WRITE_CLAUSE = /\b(?:write|writes|writing|save|saves|saving|record|records|recording|store|stores|storing|persist|persists|persisting|append|appends|appending|capture|captures|capturing|file)\b[^.]{0,48}?\b(?:note|notes|memory|memories|concept|concepts|document|documents|gap|gaps|journal|entry|entries|obsidian|vault)\b/i; const isCompositionAsk = WRITE_CLAUSE.test(goal);` — computed unconditionally after the decisionCache lookup. A byte-identical HAND-DUPLICATED copy lives at :222 as `_COMPOSITION_WRITE_CLAUSE`, used by deterministicCompositionAsk (:227) and deterministicEnvGateRoute (:276).
- **Consumer:** FOUR readers, all as `!isCompositionAsk && ...`: :503 (extract-from-source → shellResult @0.6), :522 (registry/inventory count → shellResult @0.6), :537 (filesystem aggregate → shellResult @0.6), :557 (substrate-data aggregate → shellResult @0.6). NON-readers on the same code path: :419-441 the EXPLICITLY-NAMED SHAPE route (`/\b(run|runs|execute|executes|invoke|invokes|perform|performs|trigger|triggers)\b\s+(?:the\s+|a\s+|an\s+)?([\w\s-]{3,40})/i` matched against a derived shape phrase → single shape @0.8) and :459-483 the PROSE route (EXPLANATORY_RE && !NOT_PROSE_RE && !LIVE_MEASUREMENT_RE → single shape @0.7). Both sit ABOVE the four guarded shortcuts and return early.
- **Value form:** boolean on both sides; the regex is the real interface and it exists in two textually separate copies (:222 and :395) with no shared constant. NOT_PROSE_RE at :478 contains its own independent persist alternation (`write (a|the)? ?(note|file|concept)|save|store|persist`) — a THIRD, differently-worded copy of the same intent.
- **Suspected:** SUSPECTED LIVE BYPASS — this is the seam the stretch names. Concrete failure: "Run the test suite for repos/goal-host-vessel and record the pass count in a memory note" hits _namedShape on `run the test suite` → returns `{shapes:["test_suite"], confidence:0.8}` at :436, never consulting isCompositionAsk. The plan is the truncated set, the walk produces exactly it, and the reach gate — which grades against the plan — greens while no note was written. That is verbatim the failure the guard's own comment at :373-394 documents as "observed live". The prose route at :481 is partially covered by NOT_PROSE_RE's persist terms, but only for the exact wordings it enumerates ("write a note" yes; "record it in a memory note" — `record` is absent from NOT_PROSE_RE — no). AGREEMENT: the three regex copies agree today; drift is unobserved by any test that compares them.

### `R1-infer-10` — inferredTargetShapeCache — one Map<string,string[]> serving two key namespaces, one of them dead

- **Datum:** Two unrelated cached values sharing one Map: (a) target shapes keyed by 8-hex goal hash, (b) the TERMINAL half of a derivation split keyed by `split:<8hex>:<sorted-comma-joined-inferred-targets>`.
- **Producer:** (a) goal-target-inference.ts:195-201 `cache.set(goalHashOf(goal), filtered)` inside inferGoalTargetShapes — a function with NO caller (index.ts:246 imports it; the only other mention is the comment at index.ts:3503; the two-arg function is otherwise unreferenced in src/). (b) goal-target-inference.ts:883-889 `cache.set(\`split:${goalHashOf(goal)}:${inferredTargets.slice().sort().join(",")}\`, terminal)` — fires only on the LLM split path, after both intermediate and terminal are non-empty (:875).
- **Consumer:** (b) goal-target-inference.ts:795-802: on hit, `const term = new Set(cached); return {intermediate: inferredTargets.filter(s => !term.has(s)), terminal: cached.slice()}` — the intermediate list is RECONSTRUCTED BY SET-DIFFERENCE, not stored. Both index.ts call sites pass the same Map object: :10957 `cache: inferredTargetShapeCache` into inferGoalTargetDecision (which never reads `opts.cache` — it reads only `opts.decisionCache`), and :11074 into inferDerivationSplit (which does).

Both live in one `Map<string,string[]>` with a shared 512-entry FIFO evict — an (a) write can evict a (b) entry and vice versa. The reconstruction at :800 is only correct if `inferredTargets` is IDENTICAL to the set that was hashed into the key; the key includes the sorted set, so this holds — but the reconstruction loses the LLM's `intermediate` ORDER, which :1112 (`[...split.intermediate, ...terminalOutputShapes]`) depends on to sequence production.
- **Suspected:** AGREEMENT ok but the (a) namespace is DEAD: inferGoalTargetShapes has no call site, so `cache:` at index.ts:10957 is an inert argument. Note the header comment at goal-target-inference.ts:17 shows an orphaned `const MAX_TARGET_SHAPES = 3;` line that fell INSIDE the module docstring — evidence of a prior mechanical edit through this region. The order-loss on a split cache HIT is a real behavioural divergence between a cold and a warm dispatch for the same goal.

### `R1-override-11` — post-inference overrides mutate seededOutputShapes/goalTargetDecision without writing back to decisionCache

- **Datum:** The FINAL target-shape set and its confidence — after four in-process overrides applied downstream of inference.
- **Producer:** index.ts, all inside the `if (!seededOutputShapes || seededOutputShapes.length === 0)` block. (1) :10986-10994 countable-goal UNION — condition `isCountableQuestion(goal) && knownShapes.includes("shellResult") && seededOutputShapes.length > 0 && !seededOutputShapes.includes("shellResult")` → `seededOutputShapes = [...seededOutputShapes, "shellResult"]` (does NOT touch goalTargetDecision). (2) :11005-11025 SHELL SAFETY NET — condition `knownShapes.includes("shellResult") && (!seededOutputShapes || length===0)` AND imperativeInspect && inspectNoun && !writeAnalysisEdit → sets BOTH `seededOutputShapes = ["shellResult"]` and `goalTargetDecision = {shapes:["shellResult"], confidence:0.5, alternatives:[]}`. (3) :11030-11034 PRODUCER-LOOKUP OVERRIDE — condition `knownShapes.includes("shellResult") && parseProducerLookup(goal)`, UNCONDITIONAL otherwise (overwrites a good inference) → `{shapes:["shellResult"], confidence:0.7, alternatives:[]}`. (4) :11043-11058 COMPUTE-CHAIN AUGMENTATION — condition source-verb && derive-verb && write-verb && inferred has a source shape && a write shape && no shellResult → `seededOutputShapes = [..._src, ..._rest, "shellResult", ..._wr].slice(0,3)` and rebuilds goalTargetDecision preserving the old confidence.
- **Consumer:** index.ts:5448 and :14633 read CONFIDENCE from `inferredTargetDecisionCache`, which none of these four writes. index.ts:11386/:12256 read SHAPES from `seededOutputShapes`, which all four do write. index.ts:11472/:12225 read ALTERNATIVES from `goalTargetDecision`, which overrides (2) and (3) blank to `[]`.
- **Value form:** seededOutputShapes: `string[]` (post-override, up to 3 after (4)'s slice). goalTargetDecision.confidence: 0.5 / 0.7 / carried-over. Cached confidence: whatever `remember()` stored BEFORE any override, or absent.
- **Suspected:** AGREEMENT SPLIT: the shapes plane and the confidence plane diverge after this point. `inference_confidence` persisted to goal_execution_paths (:5448) and surfaced on the dispatch record (:14633) describes a decision that overrides (2), (3) and (4) may have replaced — so any correlation of confidence against reach is measuring a superseded decision. Override (3) is UNCONDITIONAL by design (comment at :11026) and blanks alternatives, killing the reframe retry at :11472 for every producer-lookup goal. Override (4)'s `.slice(0,3)` can drop the write terminal it just re-ordered when `_rest` is non-empty.

### `R1-split-12` — inferDerivationSplit → {intermediate, terminal} → seededOutputShapes + terminalOutputShapes (+ vault-note remap)

- **Datum:** `DerivationSplit = {intermediate: string[]; terminal: string[]}` — which inferred shapes are produced FIRST and which are deferred emit terminals whose content is bound from the intermediates.
- **Producer:** goal-target-inference.ts:765-894. Exits: :773 noSplit when `inferredTargets.length < 2`; :782 DETERMINISTIC split when `emit.length>=1 && nonEmit.length>=1 && !inferredTargets.includes("activityVariant_write")` where EMIT_TERMINALS = {memoryNote_write, concept_write, substrateGap_write}; :784 noSplit when no LLM; :791 noSplit for activityVariant_write; :795-802 cache hit; :826/:835/:840/:892 noSplit on LLM failure; :875 noSplit when either side empty; :890 the LLM split after the RAW_SOURCE_SHAPES inversion guard (:858-872) and the problem_detection-over-code_quality swap (:878-882). `noSplit = {intermediate: [], terminal: inferredTargets.slice()}`.

Then the terminal set is REMAPPED: `UNRESOLVABLE_FS_WRITE = new Set(["fileWriteResult","fileEditResult","fs_write","fs_edit"])`, remapped to `"obsidian:write_note"` when `wantsVaultNote` (`/substrate\/[^\s"']*\.md\b/i` OR `/\bnotes?\b/i` on the goal) AND `shapeEndpointMap.has("obsidian:write_note") || discoveredProxyShapes.includes("obsidian:write_note")`. The DETERMINISTIC branch at :782 puts EVERY non-emit shape in intermediate — including shellResult — so a 3-shape [http_fetch, shellResult, memoryNote_write] splits as intermediate=[http_fetch, shellResult]. Consumer: seededOutputShapes concatenated intermediates-then-terminals; the walk's deferral reads terminalOutputShapes as a membership set. `wantsVaultNote`'s `/\bnotes?\b/i` alternative matches ANY goal containing the word "note" — including "note that X".

Two hazards: (a) the remap key is `"obsidian:write_note"` — a colon-bearing shape name that must match byte-exactly in BOTH `shapeEndpointMap` and `discoveredProxyShapes`, which are populated from two different sources; (b) the alternative-framing retry at :11489 passes `terminalOutputShapes: undefined`, so a reframed derivation loses its deferral and the terminal can be satisfied directly from goal text — hollow-note by construction. Also: the LLM split's `terminal` order is discarded on a cache hit (see R1-infer-10).

[]` at :8235 means an undefined target is an EMPTY set (opportunistic mode), not an error.

Because the rename erases provenance, the walk's honest-reach machinery grades against a plan it cannot distinguish from an operator contract, and the `?? []` coalesce makes "inference produced nothing" and "caller declared nothing" the same state at the consumer. ### `R1-store-14` — recordGoalPath → POST /v2/goal-paths → goal_execution_paths (goal_hash re-derived; two hash identities; option<> binds)

- **Datum:** The learning record of this intake decision: goal_text, expected_output_shapes (the inferred target set), inference_confidence, path_activities, success.

- **Consumer:** activity-api/src/routes/goal-paths.ts:407-757. `PathRecordRequestSchema.parse(body)` (:410); `const goalHash = hashGoal(validated.goal_text)` (:412) — the store RE-DERIVES the hash and ignores anything goal-host computed. - **Value form:** TWO INCOMPATIBLE goal_hash IDENTITIES for the same goal. goal-host `goalHashOf` (goal-target-inference.ts:28-46): NFC → lowercase → `\s+`→" " → trim → FNV-1a 32-bit → **8 lowercase hex**. activity-api `hashGoal` (goal-paths.ts:135-179): lowercase → trim → elide `\b[0-9a-f]{8,}(?::\d+)?\b` containing a digit → "id" → elide `\b\d{10,}\b` → "n" → strip `[^\w\s]` → spaces→"_" → md5 → **16 lowercase hex**. Request field `inference_confidence` (number|null) maps to stored column `last_inference_confidence` (`option<number>`, migration 156). `expected_output_shapes` is `option<array<string>>` (migrations 188/189/190).

This is the '8-hex digest read by a 16-hex regex' class named in the brief. SEPARATELY, suspected latent NULL-vs-NONE: goal-paths.ts:686 binds JS `null` into `option<array<string>>` on the CREATE path — the exact class the 2026-08-21 report measured as a SILENT no-write on CREATE. Today's caller never triggers it (recordGoalPath's signature at index.ts:5427 defaults `expectedOutputShapes: string[] = []` and all seven call sites pass an array), so agreement holds CONDITIONALLY ON THIS CALLER; the bind is latently lossy for any future/other caller that omits the field, and the CREATE would return an empty result set with no error while the caller logs success. LIVENESS: UNOBSERVABLE-PLANE-MASKED for the producer (goal-host masked); the consumer (activity-api) IS running. Probe once unmasked: POST /v2/impulses/resolve {pointer:{type:"db_admin",operation:"diagnose",tables:["goal_execution_paths"]}} and compare max(updated_at) against the masking timestamp 2026-08-16 21:13, then count rows with expected_output_shapes = NONE vs [] to separate 'never sent' from 'sent empty'.

### `R2-select-01` — Backward-chain recursion: unsatisfied producer inputs become sub-targets

- **Datum:** missing input shape names added to the walk's `target` Set (in-process, plain strings)
- **Producer:** repos/goal-host-vessel/src/index.ts:9105-9112 (candidate path) and :9149-9158 (backward-chain path) — `for (const s of needsInputs.inputShapes) if (!producedShapes.has(s) && !target.has(s)) { target.add(s); added = true; }` then `continue`. Fires ONLY when no `pick` was found AND some candidate/producer both `advancesTarget` and has `inputShapes.length > 0`, AND at least one of those inputs is neither produced nor already a target (`added`); if nothing is added the loop does NOT continue and falls through to mint/stop.
- **Consumer:** same file, next iteration: `missingTargetsB = [...target].filter(s => !producedShapes.has(s))` (:8817), `advancesTarget` (:8821), and the (c) backward-chain request body `required_shapes: missingTargets` (:9131). Unconditional per iteration.
- **Value form:** producer writes bare shape names as read from `WalkCandidate.inputShapes`, which is `declaredInputs.filter(s => !optionalInputShapes.includes(s))` (index.ts:6013-6015) — i.e. AFTER optional subtraction. Consumer reads the same Set. Because seam R2-select-08 never receives `optional_shapes` through discover, an optional (non-gating) input is recursed on as a hard sub-target.
- **Suspected:** AGREEMENT ok in-process. Coupled defect: optional inputs become mandatory sub-targets because the optional list is empty by construction on the discover path (see R2-select-07). LIVENESS UNOBSERVABLE-PLANE-MASKED (goal-host masked). Probe once unmasked: `journalctl -u goal-host-vessel | grep -c 'recurse —\|backward-chain —'` over a window with dispatches, versus walk count.

- **Datum:** the candidate admission set: activity rows whose declared shapes intersect required_shapes
- **Producer:** repos/activity-api/src/services/discover-by-shapes.ts:219-237 — forward: `output_shapes CONTAINSANY $required_shapes`; backward: `input_shapes CONTAINSANY $required_shapes` (+ optional `output_shapes CONTAINSANY $output_shapes_filter`); both AND `(proposed = false OR proposed IS NONE)` (:217) and a retired clause that is REWRITTEN at :234 by `.replace(/AND \(retired = false OR retired IS NONE\)/g, 'AND retired = false')`; `ORDER BY ev DESC, created_at DESC LIMIT $admission_limit` with ADMISSION_CAP=1000 (:196-197).

- **Datum:** one Beta(α,β) draw per candidate, and the response array ORDER that results from it
- **Producer:** repos/activity-api/src/services/discover-by-shapes.ts:311-326 — `sampled_score = betaSample(a.metrics?.thompson_alpha ?? 1, a.metrics?.thompson_beta ?? UNTRIED_PRIOR_BETA)` attached to every legacy activity, unconditionally (both modes, since FM-2). Then :433 `finalActivities.sort((a,b) => (b.sampled_score??0) - (a.sampled_score??0))` and :438 `slice(0, limit)`.
- **Consumer:** repos/goal-host-vessel/src/index.ts:9098 (`[...candidates].sort((a,b) => scaffoldRank(a,target) - scaffoldRank(b,target)).find(feasibleProducer …)`), :9089 (reuse pick), :8945 (OR-edge sort). Every genuine producer returns scaffoldRank 0 (producer-pick.ts:19 `if (!isHollowScaffold(c.id)) return 0;`) and JS sort is stable — so among genuine producers the winner is whichever came FIRST in the response array.
- **Value form:** `sampled_score` is a top-level number on the wire. Server-side the sort key is the raw global draw ONLY — the composition blend and ψ are attached to the same objects but never enter this sort. Client-side the blend result lives in `WalkCandidate.sampledScore` but is not a sort key for genuine producers.
- **Suspected:** CONFIRMED structurally: the discover response order IS the pick order for genuine producers, and that order is the pure global Thompson draw. Edge blend (R2-select-08) and ψ (R2-select-10) therefore cannot reorder a genuine producer on this path. Note the interaction with the cap: the draw runs over ≤1000 admitted rows, then truncates to limit=50 — sample-then-truncate is correct, but the ORDER BY ev cap still precedes it.

### `R2-select-07` — transformToLegacyTemplate — the key-by-key projection that decides what reaches the walk (drops optional_input_shapes)

- **Datum:** the candidate record as the walk sees it: variant_id/activity_id/input_schema/output_schema/metrics
- **Producer:** repos/activity-api/src/db/paradigm.ts:980-1002 (`transformToLegacyTemplate`), called at services/discover-by-shapes.ts:313 with `rest` (comp_row stripped). It emits `input_schema.optional_shapes` ONLY when `activity.optional_input_shapes?.length > 0` (paradigm.ts:990-994).
- **Consumer:** repos/goal-host-vessel/src/index.ts:6012 — `optionalInputShapes = norm(x.optional_input_shapes ?? x.optionalInputShapes ?? x.input_schema?.optional_shapes)`; used at :6015 to subtract optional inputs from the gating input set.
- **Value form:** The SELECT that feeds the transform (services/discover-by-shapes.ts:232) projects `id, name, description, category, tasks, scope, org_id, input_shapes, output_shapes, ev, created_at, updated_at` — `optional_input_shapes` is NOT among them, although the column exists and is written (db/paradigm.ts:263, routes/activities.ts:568-569). So `activity.optional_input_shapes` is always undefined on this path and `input_schema.optional_shapes` can never be emitted. Also dropped: `id` (the walk falls back to `activity_id`/`variant_id`, which carry the RecordId), `tags`, `proposed`, `retired`, `ev`.
- **Suspected:** CONFIRMED AGREEMENT FAILURE, discover path only (statically provable: column absent from the SELECT list). Consequence: every declared-optional input is treated by the walk as gating, so `inputsSatisfied`/`feasibleProducer` reject producers that are in fact cold-feasible — and the substrate's own reach-gap remedy text (index.ts:5085, "declare the gating input as optional_input_shapes") is inert when the candidate arrives through discover. Check the /recommend path separately: recommendations emit `input_schema: template.input_schema` verbatim (activities.ts:6708), so that route may carry it.

### `R2-select-08` — Response → readCandidateShapes → blendEdgeScore (composition_score object → WalkCandidate.sampledScore)

- **Datum:** WalkCandidate {id, inputShapes, outputShapes, alpha, beta, sampledScore, sampleCount}
- **Producer:** discover/recommend response fields: `sampled_score`, `metrics.thompson_alpha/beta`, `composition_score` (object), `input_schema.required_shapes`, `output_schema.produces_shapes`.
- **Consumer:** repos/goal-host-vessel/src/index.ts:6003-6041 `readCandidateShapes(x, k)`, called at :8484, :8750, :8770, :8804, :8933, :9138 — always with `_blendK` (index.ts:8378) EXCEPT the default-parameter path. Reads `id = String(x.template_id || x.id || x.activity_id || x.variant_id)`; `globalScore = numOr(x.sampled_score ?? x.sampledScore ?? x.thompson_score ?? x.score) ?? betaSample(alpha,beta)`; `edge = x.composition_score as EdgeEvidence`; `sampledScore = blendEdgeScore(globalScore, edge, betaSample(edge.alpha, edge.beta), k)` (edge-blend.ts:78-90, weight = samples/(samples+k), 0 when sample_count≤0 or α/β non-numeric).
- **Value form:** `composition_score` is an OBJECT on both sides (the earlier `typeof v === 'number'` bug is fixed — the fields are read individually via `numOr(edge?.alpha)`). `id` is `String()` of whatever survives; on the discover path `id` was dropped by the transform, so it resolves to `activity_id`, a SurrealDB RecordId serialized by JSON — canonical `activity:⟨name⟩`. `sampleCount` is read from `x.sample_count ?? x.metrics?.sample_count`, neither of which discover emits (the edge count lives inside `composition_score.sample_count`), so `WalkCandidate.sampleCount` is always undefined from discover.
- **Suspected:** AGREEMENT ok for α/β/sampled_score/composition_score; `sample_count` top-level is a dead read. The blend is a strict no-op whenever composition_score is null — which is the standing case while the edge join is child-global and the graph is a stale batch (R2-select-04). VISIBILITY: nothing counts how many candidates arrived with non-null composition_score; a zero-weight blend and an absent field are indistinguishable in the walk's logs.

### `R2-select-09` — policies/selection-tuning.json → edgeBlendK / maxWalkSteps / horizontalK (file → in-process, 30s TTL)

- **Datum:** selection tuning numbers read at use time
- **Producer:** an authored JSON file at `${WORKSPACE_ROOT}/policies/selection-tuning.json` (repos/goal-host-vessel/src/selection-tuning.ts:51 `selectionTuningPath`). Tier order: authored row → env (`GOAL_HOST_WALK_MAX_STEPS`, `GOAL_HOST_HORIZONTAL_K`; edgeBlendK has NO env tier, :81) → compiled default (edgeBlendK=EDGE_BLEND_K=10, maxWalkSteps=40, horizontalK=4).
- **Consumer:** repos/goal-host-vessel/src/index.ts:8378 `const _blendK = (await resolveSelectionTuning()).edgeBlendK` (once per walk, threaded into every readCandidateShapes call); :6150 `MAX_STEPS`; :8956 `horizontalK`.

Probe: `docker exec substrate-live sh -c 'ls -l $WORKSPACE_ROOT/policies/selection-tuning.json'` — absence means every walk ran on compiled defaults, which is expected, not broken. Note the readCandidateShapes default parameter (index.ts:6003) means an unthreaded call site would silently use the compiled K; all current sites pass `_blendK`.

### `R2-select-10` — ψ inputs: dev-vessel state signature → psiInputs → sigUsable gate (discover) AND the SF-blend completion_shapes conjunct (recommend)

- **Datum:** {signature, completion_shapes} — the (s, R) pair that unlocks ⟨ψ(s,a),R⟩
- **Producer:** repos/development-vessel/src/resolvers/compute-state-signature.ts:223-228 `computeHash` = `createHash('sha1').update(json).digest('hex').slice(0,8)` → an 8-hex sha1 over load/memory/trace counters. (b) repos/activity-api/src/routes/activities.ts:6836-6844 — the SF blend requires `completion_shapes.length > 0` (the signature itself is derived server-side at :6180, so the blocking conjunct is completion_shapes).

Second-order visibility defect: because psiInputs omits the field entirely rather than sending the foreign value, the store's loud `sf_signature_namespace_mismatch` warn (discover-by-shapes.ts:361-368) can never fire either — the miss is silent at every hop. Probe once goal-host is unmasked: `journalctl -u activity-api | grep -c 'successor-features blend applied'` should be 0, and every discover response should lack `successor_value`.

### `R2-select-13` — scaffoldRank / pathwaySet — how sampledScore and the reuse pathway actually enter the pick

- **Datum:** the rank integer (−1/0/1) that orders candidates, and the pathway id set that tie-breaks
- **Producer:** repos/goal-host-vessel/src/producer-pick.ts:15-36 `scaffoldRank(c, targetShapes)` — returns 0 for any non-hollow id; for a hollow scaffold: 1 if it does not cover a target shape, −1 if it covers one AND `typeof c.sampledScore === 'number' && c.sampledScore > 0.5`, else 0. `pathwaySet` is built at index.ts:6123 from `opts.preferPathway` (from POST /v2/goal-paths/recommend, index.ts:5582) via `normActivityId`.
- **Consumer:** repos/goal-host-vessel/src/index.ts:9089-9090 (reuse pick: `.sort(scaffoldRank)` then `.find(c => pathwaySet.has(normActivityId(c.id)) && feasibleProducer(c) && scaffoldRank(c,target) <= 0)`), :9098 (primary pick), :8945 (OR-edge ordering), :8577 (satisfier reuse, matching `satisfier:<shape>` strings against pathwaySet).
- **Value form:** `sampledScore` (the blended number) is consumed ONLY as the boolean threshold `> 0.5`, and only for ids matching `/^(compose-|learned-compose|proposed_pattern_authored_|repaired-)/` or containing `-to-`. Genuine producers never consult it. pathwaySet keys are `normActivityId`-stripped bare ids, plus the literal `satisfier:<shape>` form for satisfier steps.
- **Suspected:** CONFIRMED structurally: all learned evidence (global draw, edge blend, ψ) collapses to a single binary on the hollow-scaffold channel; for genuine producers the ordering is entirely the server's response order (R2-select-06). Value-form risk on pathwaySet: `preferPathway` ids come from the goal-paths store and are normalized, while satisfier steps must match the `satisfier:` prefix verbatim — a store that records satisfier steps in any other form matches nothing and reuse silently never fires.

### `R2-select-14` — ias-executor producer_selection resolver → discover-by-shapes (slot-binding's select_or_produce)

- **Datum:** chosen_producer {activity_id, score} + `unbindable` flag for the missing shape
- **Producer:** repos/ias-executor-ts/src/resolvers/producer-selection.ts:63-79 — POSTs `{output_shapes:[shape], mode:'candidates_with_scores'}` to `${endpoint}/v2/activities/discover-by-shapes`, only when both `endpoint` and `apiKey` are configured (:104-108); otherwise `httpAttempted=false` and unbindable is declared without asking.
- **Consumer:** same file :80-82 `const data = await res.json() as {producers?, candidates?}; return data.producers ?? data.candidates ?? []`, then :113-121 `chosen = producers[0]; unbindable = chosen == null; score: chosen.composition_score?.alpha ?? null`. Downstream: slot-binding's escalate_unbindable / agent_fill_fallback / forge_missing_shape branches key on `metadata.unbindable`.
- **Value form:** THREE independent mismatches in series: (1) the request omits `required_shapes`, which `validateDiscoverByShapesInput` (services/discover-by-shapes.ts:107-113) rejects with HTTP 400 → `!res.ok` → `[]`; (2) even on success the route returns `{activities, total}` (activities.ts:5774), never `producers` or `candidates` → `[]`; (3) `score` is taken as the raw `composition_score.alpha` (a Beta α count, α = success_count+1), not a probability or a Thompson draw.
- **Suspected:** CONFIRMED AGREEMENT FAILURE, statically provable: whenever the HTTP call is attempted this resolver reports `unbindable: true`, and the failure is indistinguishable from a genuine capability gap (`degraded: true` is set unconditionally, so the honest marker never discriminates). LIVENESS: I did not establish who invokes it — registered at repos/ias-executor-ts/src/hosts/goal-host.ts:61 and examples/goal-host.ts:61. Probe: search traces for tasks of type `producer_selection` and count `metadata.unbindable` true vs false — an all-true population confirms.

## LEARNING 2 — minting structure from observation (traces → new informational structure: composition edges, ribosome-extracted templates, variant mints, filed gaps)


> FIFTEENTH AND SIXTEENTH SEAMS, cut for the 14-item cap — both are gap-filing and both are first-class; include them if the cap is lifted.

(A) L2-structure-15 — substrateGap_write → the gap store FILE.
  Datum: a `SubstrateGap` {id, category, source, status, detected_at, summary, classification_metadata, first_detected_at, closed_by_trace?}.
  Producer: ~30 detectors in repos/development-vessel/src/resolvers/* and /seed/* call `resolveSubstrateGapWrite` — e.g. activity-lifecycle-audit.ts:315 (`category:"activity_lifecycle"`), detector-coverage-scan.ts:150 (`category:"detector_coverage_gap"`), vessel-exercise-scan.ts:77 (`category:"vessel_exercise_zero_coverage"`), obsidian-request-scan.ts:281 (`category:"systematic_failure"`), gap-to-feature.ts (~20 sites).
  Consumer: repos/development-vessel/src/resolvers/substrate-gap.ts:315 `resolveSubstrateGapWrite`. FOUR validation gates, each returning `{shape:"structuredError"}` rather than writing: missing `gap` (:320-333), missing/empty `gap.id` (:352-365 — "the field is `id`, not `gap_id`"), empty summary or `{{` in id/category on an OPEN gap (:370-386), and a timestamp-placeholder SCRUB (:400-401) that silently rewrites unbound `{{…}}` timestamps to the server clock.
  Store: a FILE, not a table — `GAPS_PATH() = join(workspaceRoot(), "gaps", "gaps.json")` (:150), read at :202, written tmp+rename at :236-238 with a per-write unique tmp name.
  LIVE EVIDENCE: on the hub, `/workspace/gaps/gaps.json` is 29,998 bytes, mtime **Aug 4 17:14** — 17 days stale, and the directory itself has not changed since Aug 8. development-vessel is masked, so this is a frozen store, not a live one. suspected_status: LIVENESS ZERO, and the 08-21 report's step-0 point stands — the outage blocks filing a gap about itself.
  Value-form note: `coerceFlatGapPointer` (:318) accepts a FLAT pointer carrying summary/detail/description/title and rewrites it into `pointer.gap` — a shape-repair layer whose existence is evidence that callers disagree with the contract.

(B) L2-structure-16 — gaps.json → boredom-vessel goal admission (a VALUE-DOMAIN agreement failure with identical field names on both sides).
  Producer: the categories above.
  Consumer: repos/boredom-vessel/src/goal-generation.ts:245-247 — `const ACTIONABLE_CATEGORIES = new Set(["ui_legibility"]); const categoryActionable = ACTIONABLE_CATEGORIES.has(String(g.category ?? "")); if (!categoryActionable && !/capability|repair/i.test(g.summary)) continue;`. Also `admitActionableGapsMirror` at :82-95 and a `CATEGORY_WEIGHT` sort at :127.
  Value-form: both sides use the key `category` and both use strings. The DOMAINS do not intersect — `detector_coverage_gap`, `activity_lifecycle`, `vessel_exercise_zero_coverage`, `systematic_failure`, `remedy_livelock`, `novel_failure_mode_detected` are all admitted only by accident, if their free-prose summary happens to contain "capability" or "repair". The file's own comments record the measured cost of the earlier prose-only gate: "it drops 247 of 524 open gaps (47%)" (:223). This is the rubric's canonical agreement failure: identical names, disjoint value domains, statically checkable, and no counter anywhere reports the drop rate.
  There is a further fan-out from substrate-gap.ts I did not give a seam id: on a created/reopened OPEN gap it (i) `Bun.spawn(["systemctl","start","gap-compose.service"])` — masked, verified; (ii) an in-process `fetch(DEV_VESSEL_ENDPOINT/v2/impulses/resolve, {impulse:{type:"gap_to_feature",…}})` nudge with a 600s timeout; (iii) a `poolImpulse_write` mirror under id `"gap:"+gap.id` with a PROJECTED body {gap_id, category, route, remedy, summary} — a key-by-key rebuild that drops classification_metadata, status history and detected_at; (iv) `POST ${ACTIVITY_API_ENDPOINT}/v2/events/publish` type `devvessel.gap.written` with header `X-Internal-Api-Key: development-vessel`. (iii) is a silent-dropper by the task's definition and belongs in the inventory if you have room.

CROSS-CUTTING OBSERVATIONS

1. "Success" is two different quantities inside one stretch. The edge-mint path binds `trace.success === true` (template exit status, execution-traces.ts:2364); the ribosome extraction path gates on `isHonestlyReached` (ribosome.ts:68, wrapping `classifyReach`) and goal-host's mint gates on `grounded` (index.ts:5781). The composition graph's α/β and the template corpus are therefore learned over two different labels with the same name.

2. Every write in this stretch is DETACHED and best-effort. `void … .catch(() => {})` at execution-traces.ts:2361; fire-and-forget WS emit at activities.ts:981; `onTemplateCreated(msg, replayDeps)` unawaited at ribosome-vessel index.ts:631; per-edge try/catch in the reconciler at :488. Not one of them increments a counter a consumer reads. That is the stretch's dominant visibility property, and it is what let both the derive fix (2026-08-11) and the graph freeze (2026-07-14) stay invisible.

3. My own retraction, recorded so a later validator does not repeat it: my first greps for `activityTemplate_write` and `case '` in repos/activity-api/src/routes/impulses.ts returned ZERO hits and I nearly reported the handler as missing. The file trips grep's binary heuristic; `grep -a` shows the handler at :2679. A failed/suppressed query is not an empty result.

4. Instrument caveats honored: I used only `db_admin diagnose` for row counts (sanctioned, read-only) and did NOT use `/v2/activities/composition/graph`, whose `"total":0` and non-unique `ORDER BY weight DESC` make ~19% of rows unreachable per the 08-21 report.

5. Mask state re-verified this session, not assumed: `docker exec substrate-live systemctl is-enabled goal-host-vessel development-vessel ribosome-vessel boredom-vessel activity-api composition-edge-reconcile.timer gap-compose` → masked, masked, masked, masked, **enabled**, masked, masked. Only activity-api is live, and it is a passive consumer for most of this stretch — so the whole LEARNING-2 stretch has no live producer at its head. Anything reported as "liveness observed" here without unmasking is reading a stale row.

### `L2-structure-01` — Child trace carries parent_execution_id across the vessel boundary into trace ingest

- **Datum:** The parent→child adjacency fact: `parent_execution_id` on the trace-insert body. Shape: activityExecutionTrace_write / POST /v2/execution-traces body field.
- **Producer:** repos/ias-executor-ts/src/adapters/activity-api-trace-sink.ts:178 `parent_execution_id: trace.parentExecutionId` (and the sibling projection at repos/ias-executor-ts/src/adapters/activity-api-provider.ts:271). Fires on EVERY trace flush; the field is present-but-undefined when the execution was top-level. `trace.parentExecutionId` is populated only for nested runs — set from `options.parentExecutionId` (engine.ts:162/1303/1422) or `opts.executionId` when the engine recurses into a sub-execution (engine.ts:1560/1655).
- **Consumer:** repos/activity-api/src/routes/execution-traces.ts:2360 `if (body.parent_execution_id) { void deriveCompositionEdgeFromParent(...) }`. Also read at :1875-1876 to build `resolvedCompositionChain` via `denormalizeCompositionChain`, and at :1974 to persist the column. Condition: truthy string only — an absent/empty value silently skips the entire edge-derive limb with no counter.
- **Value form:** Producer: `string | undefined`, BARE execution id (e.g. `01JXY…`) as held by the engine; `undefined` is JSON-dropped. Consumer: `body.parent_execution_id as string | undefined`; derive re-normalizes at execution-traces.ts:1613-1615 by splitting on ':' and stripping ⟨⟩, so it tolerates both bare and `execution:⟨id⟩` forms.
- **Suspected:** LIVENESS UNOBSERVABLE-PLANE-MASKED — every producer of nested traces (goal-host-vessel, development-vessel, ribosome-vessel) is masked (verified `systemctl is-enabled` → masked ×4, activity-api enabled). Probe once unmasked: `db_admin diagnose` cannot count this; instead POST /v2/impulses/resolve pointer {type:'activityExecutionTrace', …} or count traces with a non-empty `composition_chain` in the last hour at the CONSUMING side, plus a log line added at :2360.

### `L2-structure-02` — In-process 4-positional-argument projection into deriveCompositionEdgeFromParent (the amputation that makes the CREATE unsatisfiable)

- **Datum:** The four facts the edge writer is given: child activity id, parent execution id, success flag, jwt. Everything else about the earned execution — `trace.execution_id`, `traceOrgId`, `resolvedCompositionChain`, the reach verdict — is in scope at the call site and is NOT passed.
- **Producer:** repos/activity-api/src/routes/execution-traces.ts:2361-2367 — `deriveCompositionEdgeFromParent(trace.activity_id, body.parent_execution_id, trace.success === true, jwtAuth?.jwtToken)`. Fires detached (`void … .catch(() => {})`) whenever seam-01's condition holds. In scope but unpassed at that exact point: `traceOrgId` (:1849), `resolvedCompositionChain` (:2349), `trace.execution_id`.

The CREATE binds NONE of the last three.

- **Value form:** `declaredOut` is read from `output_shapes` OR `outputShapes` on the incoming object (:801-809) — two accepted key spellings, and an object using neither yields `[]`, which makes the reuse probe vacuous rather than erroring. Failure stratification at :955-960 maps HTTP status → `auth_rejected | already_exists | validation_rejected | upstream_error`. Success returns `{shape:'activityRegistryChange', body:{variantId, parentTemplateId, accepted:true}}` (:973-976) — the id is `result.id ?? result.template_id ?? ''`, i.e. an EMPTY STRING when the API returns neither.
- **Suspected:** AGREEMENT: `REUSE_BEFORE_MINT` is a process env var deciding whether a behavioral gate fires — law-1 unobservable, invisible to traces, and its default is asserted in a comment rather than in a shaped impulse. The empty-string variantId fallback is a silent-drop: `accepted:true` with no id. LIVENESS UNOBSERVABLE-PLANE-MASKED (development-vessel masked). Probe: `journalctl -u development-vessel | grep -c 'refused duplicate mint'` against mint count, plus `/proc/<MainPID>/environ` for the effective REUSE_BEFORE_MINT value — env ground truth, not the unit file.

### `L3-relevance-02` — impulse_relevance_metrics → Thompson α/β boost inside POST /recommend (the only relevance readback into selection)

- **Datum:** relevance_score − irrelevance_score per (impulse, activity_variant_id), turned into `alphaBoost` / `betaPenalty` per candidate activity (ImpulseRelevancyBoost).

Called from repos/activity-api/src/routes/activities.ts:6290 `calculateImpulseRelevancyBoosts(activityIds, loaded_impulses, decayWeightsForRelevancy)` and :6293 `discoverMissingImpulses(...)`. CONDITION: only on POST /v2/activities/recommend, and the boost only applies to impulses present in the caller-supplied `loaded_impulses` array (`isLoaded = loadedImpulses.includes(metric.impulse_id)`, impulse-relevancy.ts:105) — a caller that omits `loaded_impulses` gets every metric classified as not-loaded.

Exact-string equality — no normalization on either side. Second projection: the SELECT at :53-67 names nine columns and DROPS `times_failed`, `net_value_score`, `resolver_*` (the second SELECT at :202-207 does read net_value_score).

LIVENESS: /recommend is live (activity-api running), but the boost is only non-zero when rows exist for the candidate ids AND the caller sent loaded_impulses; goal-host (the main /recommend caller) is masked. Probe: instrument `logger.debug('Impulse relevancy metrics fetched', {metricsCount})` at impulse-relevancy.ts:74 — a metricsCount>0 line is the consumer-side count that proves this hop.

  - agreement: CONFIRMED FAIL — the inventory listed this as unverified; it is a real, settled value-form break. Stored `activity_variant_id` is BARE: the live INFO line shows `"activity":"auto-bridge-author_producer"`. The consumer queries `WHERE activity_variant_id IN $activity_ids` (utils/impulse-relevancy.ts:53-67) where `activityIds = templates.map(t => normalizeRecordId(t.id || t.variant_id))` (routes/activities.ts:6013-6015). normalizeRecordId (utils/surrealdb-types.ts:52-66) is a PASSTHROUGH for strings and a `.toString()` for RecordIds — it never strips the `activity:⟨…⟩` wrapper (contrast normalizeActivityId, db/paradigm.ts:1932-1939, which does). I verified live that recommend candidates carry NO `t.id` (None) and their `variant_id` is `'activity:⟨learned-compose-analyze-source-to-concept-to-…⟩'`, so activityIds are PREFIXED. Exact-string `IN` against bare stored ids matches nothing. Second, smaller finding: the SELECT at :53-67 projects nine columns and drops net_value_score/times_failed/resolver_* — a key-by-key projection, though the second SELECT at :202-207 does read net_value_score.
  - liveness: Structurally zero, and the consuming side IS running: 476 `POST /v2/activities/recommend` in 24h (journalctl grep -ac), every one of which calls calculateImpulseRelevancyBoosts at activities.ts:6290 — with a prefixed-id IN-list that cannot match the bare stored ids. The direct metricsCount is NOT observable today: the only instrument is logger.debug at impulse-relevancy.ts:74 and LOG_LEVEL defaults to 'info' (config.ts:237, absent from /proc/<pid>/environ, and 0 DEBUG lines in 24h), so grepping it measures the filter. PROBE that settles the count: set LOG_LEVEL=debug on activity-api (or promote :74 to info) and read `metricsCount` on a /recommend call — expect 0 today, >0 after the id-form fix.
  - visibility: The only signal on this hop is a logger.debug line that is filtered out at the running log level — proven, 0 DEBUG lines in 24 hours. There is no counter, no health check, and a fully-starved boost is arithmetically indistinguishable from 'no relevant impulses': every metric classifies as not-loaded and every boost comes out 0. The seam can be dead for weeks and nothing anywhere says so.
  - **what must happen:** Pick ONE canonical id value-form for the learning tables and normalize at BOTH ends. Concretely: in routes/activities.ts:6013 map through `normalizeActivityId` (db/paradigm.ts:1932) instead of `normalizeRecordId` so the IN-list is bare and matches what the writer stores — and simultaneously normalize at the write end in routes/activities.ts:8952 so future rows cannot drift. Backfill impulse_relevance_metrics.activity_variant_id to the bare form. Detector: promote impulse-relevancy.ts:74 from logger.debug to a counted INFO/metric `impulse_relevancy_metrics_matched` and assert non-zero over any window with >0 recommends; a nonzero-recommends/zero-matches ratio is exactly the alarm this class of break needs.

Separately, settle the three-id-form problem once (see L3-edge-11) so ψ and the composition graph key the same activity identically.

### `L3-ratchet-05` — successor_features row count → SF_BLEND flag decision (the ratchet)

- **Datum:** `count(successor_features) >= 200` → desired flag value 1, written as tuning param `SF_BLEND`.
- **Producer:** repos/activity-api/src/jobs/accelerator-flag-tick.ts:54-56 — `SELECT count() FROM successor_features GROUP ALL`. CONDITION: runs on a timer scheduled TWICE — repos/activity-api/src/index.ts:700-709 and again at :711-720, byte-identical blocks (first tick 10 min after boot, then every ACCELERATOR_FLAG_INTERVAL_MS, default 1 h). Both schedules fire; the live journal shows every `[flag-policy]` line duplicated within milliseconds.
- **Consumer:** accelerator-flag-tick.ts:58-68 — `desired = count >= 200 ? 1 : 0`; `current = await getTuningParam('SF_BLEND', undefined, 0)`; monotone ratchet `next = current === 1 ? 1 : desired`; on `next !== current` calls `writeTuningParam(flag, next)` **with no meta argument** (:63) and logs `flipped=true`.
- **Value form:** Producer: SurrealDB `count` int. Consumer: number compared to 200; the flag value crossing to L3-tuning-06 is the JS number `1`. NOTE the write at :63 passes only (name, value) — `meta` defaults to `{}`, which is exactly the input that makes L3-tuning-06 bind NULL.
- **Suspected:** CONFIRMED BROKEN, live evidence 2026-08-21: `WARN [flag-policy] SF_BLEND evidence=sf_rows=1737 value=1 flipped=true` at 06:34, 07:34 and 08:34 — each printed TWICE (the duplicate schedule). A monotone ratchet that flips every hour forever has not landed once. `GET /v2/tuning-params/SF_BLEND` returns `{"name":"SF_BLEND","value":null}` while `substrate_tuning_param` holds 4 rows — those 4 were authored through the POST route WITH updated_by/evidence, which is why they exist and the ratchet's meta-less writes do not. AGREEMENT: fine. LIVENESS: the read side (count) is live and correct; the write side never lands. VISIBILITY: a WARN line that says `flipped=true` every hour is NEGATIVE visibility — it reports a state change that did not occur.
- **VERDICT — agreement `PASS` · liveness `FAIL` · visibility `NEGATIVE` · load-bearing `high`**
  - agreement: Read side is correct: accelerator-flag-tick.ts:52-56 `SELECT count() FROM successor_features GROUP ALL`, desired = count >= 200 ? 1 : 0, monotone `next = current === 1 ? 1 : desired`, all verified verbatim. The write at :63 is `writeTuningParam(flag, next)` with NO meta argument, so meta defaults to `{}` and tuning-params.ts:138-139 binds updated_by:null / evidence:null — precisely the input that breaks L3-tuning-06. I also confirmed the duplicate scheduling: index.ts:700-709 and :711-720 are byte-identical `import('./jobs/accelerator-flag-tick').then(...)` blocks, both installing a 10-minute initial timeout and an hourly setInterval. Every `[flag-policy]` line in the live journal appears exactly twice, milliseconds apart — a doubled tick, and (worse) two concurrent read-modify-write races on the same ratchet.
  - liveness: Directly observed, mask-independent: the READ side is live and correct (activity-api is up; the count query runs and returns 1737 every hour). The WRITE side has never landed — 4 consecutive hourly ticks all report `flipped=true` on a MONOTONE ratchet, which is arithmetically impossible if the write had ever persisted, and `GET /v2/tuning-params/SF_BLEND` returns `{"name":"SF_BLEND","value":null}` (I ran it myself today). Consuming-side count = 0 landed flips, ever.
  - visibility: The worst case in the stretch. `logger.warn('[flag-policy] SF_BLEND ... flipped=true')` at :63-64 asserts a state change that DID NOT OCCUR, once an hour, twice per hour thanks to the duplicate schedule. An operator or any log-scraping check reading this line concludes the ratchet has engaged and ψ blending is on. It actively conceals the break, and it has been doing so on every tick for as long as the journal reaches back.
  - **what must happen:** Three concrete edits. (1) Delete one of the two identical `import('./jobs/accelerator-flag-tick')` blocks in repos/activity-api/src/index.ts:700-720 — the duplicate is both noise and a concurrent-write race. (2) At accelerator-flag-tick.ts:63 pass real meta: `writeTuningParam(flag, next, {updated_by: 'accelerator-flag-tick', evidence})` — the evidence string is already computed at :56 and is exactly the provenance the row is for. (3) Make the WARN honest: only log `flipped=true` AFTER a readback confirms the stored value equals `next`; on mismatch log ERROR `flag_write_lost`. Detector: assert that a monotone flag never reports flipped=true twice — a repeated flip on a ratchet is a self-evident contradiction and should fail loudly.

### `L3-tuning-06` — writeTuningParam → substrate_tuning_param (NULL vs NONE on the create branch)

- **Datum:** A tuning row `{name, value, updated_at, updated_by, evidence}` in `substrate_tuning_param` (migration 152).
- **Producer:** repos/activity-api/src/lib/tuning-params.ts:129-142 — `UPSERT substrate_tuning_param SET name=$name, \`value\`=$value, updated_at=time::now(), updated_by=$updated_by, evidence=$evidence WHERE name=$name`, bound at :138-139 as `updated_by: meta.updated_by ?? null, evidence: meta.evidence ?? null`. CONDITION: fires on any writeTuningParam call; the meta-less callers are accelerator-flag-tick.ts:43, :63, :113.
- **Consumer:** The store. `sql/migrations/152-substrate-tuning-param.surql:22-23` defines both fields `TYPE option<string>`. In SurrealDB 2.3.3 NULL and NONE are distinct: an `option<string>` rejects NULL. On the UPDATE branch (row exists) the statement is a no-op match; on the CREATE branch the UPSERT writes nothing AND raises nothing. `writeTuningParam` returns void with no readback, so the caller cannot tell (:129 `await surrealDB.query(...)` — result discarded).
- **Value form:** Producer binds JS `null`. Column expects `NONE` (absent) or a string. Identical field names on both sides — a pure VALUE-FORM disagreement.
- **Suspected:** CONFIRMED by the L3-ratchet-05 live evidence (hourly flipped=true + value:null). This is the single hop that keeps every learned flag out of the learner. Contrast: the 4 rows that DO exist came via routes/tuning-params.ts:82, which passes non-null updated_by/evidence when the caller supplied them. Fix per the 08-21 architecture doc B3: omit absent keys (or `?? NONE`) AND read the row back inside writeTuningParam, throwing on mismatch.
- **VERDICT — agreement `FAIL` · liveness `FAIL` · visibility `NEGATIVE` · load-bearing `critical`**
  - agreement: CONFIRMED BY OBSERVATION, not inference. The live journal carries the exact SurrealDB rejection: `ERROR SurrealDB query failed {"sql":"UPSERT substrate_tuning_param SET name = $name, `value` = $value, updated_at = time::now(), updated_by = $updated_by, evidence = $evidence WHERE name = $name","params":{...,"updated_by":null,"evidence":null},"error":"Found NULL for field `evidence`, with record `substrate_tuning_param:5kfon2z2hpl379dslmor`, but expected a option<string>"}`. Producer binds JS null (tuning-params.ts:138-139 `meta.updated_by ?? null, meta.evidence ?? null`); migration 152:22-23 declares both `TYPE option<string>`. Pure value-form disagreement with identical names on both sides. TWO CORRECTIONS to the inventory: (i) the rejection is raised on the UPDATE branch — the error names an EXISTING record id — not silently on create; (ii) the create branch is NOT uniformly silent-and-lossy: PROBE_D (fresh name, evidence:null) DID land and reads back 5, while PROBE_E (identical meta) did not. So 'meta-null alone determines loss' is refuted by the data; what IS established is that writes are acknowledged and lost.
  - liveness: Observed at the consuming side (the store), mask-independent — activity-api and surrealdb are both up. db_admin diagnose gives row_counts.substrate_tuning_param = EXACTLY 4, while at least eight distinct names have been written through this path. Four names acknowledged with HTTP 200 and an INFO 'tuning param authored' line read back null today: PROBE_UPSERT_TWICE_A, PROBE_B7, PROBE_C, PROBE_E. Three read back a value: PROBE_D=5, PROBE_WRITE_READ_20260820=7, TD_LAMBDA=0.536. SF_BLEND, REPAIR_SIGNATURE_CONSUME, CROSS_SIG_REPUTATION_PENALTY and EMBEDDING_PRIOR_ENABLED all read null. ATTRIBUTION: those PROBE_* POSTs were writes by a SEPARATE session at 06:42-06:43 (updated_by 'root-cause-probe'/'probe'), not mine; I made no writes. MEASURE FIRST on the residue: the PROBE_D-vs-PROBE_E divergence means the precise branch semantics are unknown — reproduce this exact `UPSERT … WHERE name = $name` against SurrealDB 2.3.3 off-production with (a) fresh name + null meta, (b) fresh name + string meta, (c) existing row + null meta, and record which of the six cells creates, updates, errors, or silently no-ops.
  - visibility: writeTuningParam (tuning-params.ts:129-142) awaits the query and DISCARDS the result — no readback, returns void. Consequently routes/tuning-params.ts:82-98 logs `INFO tuning param authored {event:'tuning_param_written'}` and returns HTTP 200 {ok:true} for writes that left no row — observed four times today. A caller, an operator, and any log-based check all see a successful authored write. This is the single hop that keeps every learned flag out of the learner, and it reports success while doing so.
  - **what must happen:** Two edits in repos/activity-api/src/lib/tuning-params.ts, both robust to the unresolved branch residue. (1) Stop binding null: build the SET clause from only the keys actually present, or bind NONE — `updated_by = $updated_by` should simply not appear when meta.updated_by is absent. (2) READ THE ROW BACK inside writeTuningParam after the UPSERT and throw when the stored `value` does not equal the value just written; the function currently returns void with no verification, which is what converts a store-level rejection into a 200. Then routes/tuning-params.ts:82 will surface a 500 instead of 'authored', and accelerator-flag-tick.ts:63 will stop reporting phantom flips. Detector: a `tuning_param_write_verify_failed` counter plus a startup assertion that every flag the ratchet manages either has a row or has never been flipped.

### `L3-tuning-07` — substrate_tuning_param → successorBlendEnabled() gate at selection time

Called at activities.ts:6838 as the second conjunct of the ψ-blend guard.

VISIBILITY: NEGATIVE at two layers — (a) getTuningParam logs the fallback only at `logger.debug` (:100); (b) the read route repos/activity-api/src/routes/tuning-params.ts:32-51 returns HTTP 200 `{name, value:null}` for BOTH an absent row (:39-42) and a query that THREW (:44-51, catch → `c.json({name, value:null}, 200)`). Any validation protocol that reads through that route cannot distinguish 'not authored' from 'the store is down'. Live: SF_BLEND, REPAIR_SIGNATURE_CONSUME, CROSS_SIG_REPUTATION_PENALTY and EMBEDDING_PRIOR_ENABLED all return value:null (2026-08-21).
- **VERDICT — agreement `PASS` · liveness `FAIL` · visibility `NEGATIVE` · load-bearing `high`**
  - agreement: Names and types agree. activities.scoring.ts:125-127 successorBlendEnabled(): env short-circuit (`process.env.SF_BLEND === '1' || 'true'`) then `(await getTuningParam('SF_BLEND', process.env.SF_BLEND, 0)) >= 1`. getTuningParam (lib/tuning-params.ts:55-107) selects `` `value` AS param_value `` behind a 30s TTL cache and a 1.5s Promise.race deadline, swallowing any error/timeout at :98-105 into the env/default fallback. Migration 152:20 declares `value TYPE float`; the consumer's `typeof === 'number' && Number.isFinite` coercion agrees. Called as the second conjunct of the ψ guard at activities.ts:6838. I checked the env-override confound the inventory did not: neither `/etc/substrate/env` nor `/proc/1668759/environ` contains SF_BLEND (only EMBEDDING_PRIOR_ENABLED=true and EMBEDDING_PRIOR_OBSERVER_ENABLED=false), so env is not silently forcing this on or off — the gate genuinely depends on the dead write path.
  - liveness: Observed. The read executes on every /recommend (476 in 24h) and every read resolves to the fallback 0, because no SF_BLEND row exists (db_admin: substrate_tuning_param = 4 rows; GET SF_BLEND → value:null, run today) and env is unset. Zero learned flag values have ever crossed this seam into selection. The consuming-side count that proves it is the downstream one at L3-psi-10: 0 'successor-features blend applied' lines over 476 recommends.
  - visibility: Negative at TWO layers, both verified. (a) getTuningParam logs the fallback only at logger.debug (:100), and DEBUG is suppressed at the running level — 0 DEBUG lines in 24h — so a store outage silently degrades every tunable to its compiled-in default with no trace. (b) routes/tuning-params.ts:32-51 returns HTTP 200 {name, value:null} for BOTH an absent row (:39-42) and a query that THREW (:44-51, catch → c.json(...,200)). Any validation protocol reading through that route — including the one that produced this inventory — cannot distinguish 'never authored' from 'the store is down'. All four learning flags read null today, so that route currently carries near-zero information.
  - **what must happen:** Make absence distinguishable from failure. In repos/activity-api/src/routes/tuning-params.ts:44-51 return HTTP 503 (or 200 with an explicit `{status:'store_error'}` field) on the catch branch instead of the same {value:null} an absent row produces. In lib/tuning-params.ts:98-105 promote the fallback path from logger.debug to a counted WARN carrying the reason (timeout | query_error | absent), so a store outage that silently reverts every tunable to its default is visible at the default log level. Neither change fixes the flag — L3-tuning-06 does — but without them the fix cannot be verified through the only read route the system exposes.

Consumer regex demands SIXTEEN hex (a sha256 slice over shapes|provenance|missing). Two unrelated namespaces sharing the word 'signature'; no width fix reconciles them. The four-way conjunct at :6836-6844 is confirmed, and two of its clauses are independently dead — the amputated-in-series pattern.
