A mirror of the same gate exists in paradigm.ts:378-420 for the direct-insert path.
- **Value form:** Producer: a string field on the template, one of 'system' | 'learning' | 'unclassified'. Consumer: strict `=== 'system'`. A lookup FAILURE degrades to 'unclassified' (fail-open into the learning path) — the safe direction; but a MIS-classification as 'system' silently removes an arm from the bandit forever with a single INFO line at :2091.
- **VERDICT — agreement `PASS` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `NEGATIVE` · load-bearing `high`**
  - agreement: The enum agrees on both sides and the inventory's scope framing is right. resolveLearningTrack is consulted at execution-traces.ts:2072-2077 with a catch that degrades to 'unclassified' (fail-open into learning — the safe direction); `learningTrack === 'system'` at :2079 writes a thin execution_system_traces row and RETURNS at :2096-2100, skipping signature stamping (:2110), applyOutcomeToPosteriors (:2945), successor features (:3037), the v0 bucket (:3084), updateShapeActivityScores (:3412) and — confirmed — the authoritative `execution` insert at :2670, since that code is below the return. The mirror gate in paradigm.ts:378-420 does the same for the direct-insert path and returns null. The seam is the SCOPE: a storage-volume routing decision silences six learning sinks and removes the row from the authoritative trace store, and a mis-classification is one INFO line (:2091) away from retiring an arm from the bandit permanently.
  - **probe:** `SELECT count() FROM execution_system_traces GROUP ALL` versus `SELECT count() FROM execution GROUP ALL` over the same window, plus `SELECT activity_id, count() FROM execution_system_traces GROUP BY activity_id ORDER BY count DESC LIMIT 20`. HEALTHY = the enumerated activity_ids are all genuine machinery ticks and none is a template you would ever want Thompson to select.
  - visibility: LOUD: the handler returns HTTP 200 `{success:true, execution_id, stored:'system_traces'}`. At the caller — every trace poster in the fleet — that is byte-indistinguishable from a fully graded ingest. A poster whose template was mis-classified 'system' sees permanent success while none of its executions ever reaches the learner or the authoritative trace store.
  - **what must happen:** repos/activity-api/src/routes/execution-traces.ts:2096-2100 — return a distinguishable body (`stored:'system_traces', graded:false`) so a poster can assert it. DETECTOR: a periodic activity that reads `SELECT DISTINCT activity_id FROM execution_system_traces` and flags any id that also appears in the recommend candidate pool — a template that is selectable but ungradeable is the failure this gate can cause.

### `L1-credit-06` — tags → classifyReach → the three-way credit/blame/abstain decision (light-dispatch successes are permanently ungraded)

- **Datum:** `tags: string[]` carrying the walk's persisted reach verdict, and the ReachVerdict derived from it.
- **Producer:** Two producers. (a) repos/goal-host-vessel/src/index.ts:14525-14547 builds `effectiveTags = [...tags, state_signature:<hash>, mitosis*, 'dispatcher_used:goal-host', operator:<id>]` — no reach tag at dispatch time. (b) repos/activity-api/src/routes/execution-traces.ts:4498 POST /reach mirrors `array::union(tags, ['reached:true'|'reached:false'])` onto the authoritative `execution` row — fires only when a caller POSTs /reach. light-dispatch-vessel/src/index.ts:872-876 emits `dispatcher_used:light-dispatch` and never calls /reach.
- **Consumer:** repos/activity-api/src/lib/reach-classify.ts:45-63 `classifyReach`, invoked at posterior-update.ts:836 (leaf credit), execution-traces.ts:2936 (the shared verdict for the whole side-effect cluster), :3075 (v0 bucket) and :4413 (late grading). Precedence: 'reached:true' → reached; 'reached:false' → not-reached; satisfier satellite → ungraded; `dispatcher_used:goal-host` → ungraded; else `success ? 'ungraded' : 'not-reached'`.
- **Value form:** Producer writes bare colon-delimited strings into a flat array. Consumer uses `tags.includes(...)` — exact byte match, order-insensitive, no normalization. `ungraded` maps to `{alphaDelta:0, betaDelta:0}` at posterior-update.ts:844-846, which then suppresses the VPM update, the v1 cts write and the cluster mirror through their shared `(alphaDelta!==0||betaDelta!==0)` guards.
- **VERDICT — agreement `FAIL` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `WEAK` · load-bearing `critical`**
  - agreement: The tag strings agree byte-for-byte (producers write bare colon-delimited strings; classifyReach uses tags.includes with no normalization), and reach-classify.ts:11-42 documents the asymmetry deliberately, naming light-dispatch-dispatched machinery ticks as never calling POST /reach — which I confirmed (`grep -n reach repos/light-dispatch-vessel/src/index.ts` returns nothing). But I mark this FAIL, and CORRECT the inventory in the harsher direction. The inventory says 'a FAILURE penalizes β while a SUCCESS moves nothing'. At the leaf, a light-dispatch failure ALSO moves nothing: light-dispatch hardcodes `resolver_tier:"deterministic"` on every task record (index.ts:727 and :802) regardless of the actual resolver; posterior-update.ts:858-877 maps that to {resolver:'bash'}; classifyTemplateTiers returns 'all_deterministic' (tier-classifier.ts:96); and skipVariantUpdate then suppresses BOTH the VPM update (:890) and the v1 cts write (:972). Only chain credit still fires (:1125), which is blame-only for this producer and is moot for root executions with an empty chain — the ingest comment at execution-traces.ts:1911-1917 puts light-dispatch at ~10,166 ROOT executions per 72h. NET: the fleet's highest-volume producer moves no posterior anywhere, in either direction.
  - **probe:** Over a window with real walks on the local stack: `SELECT count() FROM execution WHERE tags CONTAINS 'reached:true' GROUP ALL`, `… 'reached:false'`, `… 'reach_graded:true'`, each versus `SELECT count() FROM execution GROUP ALL`. HEALTHY = the graded fraction tracks the walk fraction. Independently, take two snapshots of `SELECT variant_id, thompson_alpha, thompson_beta FROM variant_performance_metrics` ~3 min apart while dispatching light-dispatch templates and count rows that moved — HEALTHY = non-zero; expect exactly zero on HEAD.
  - visibility: `skipped_reason:'ungraded_reach'` rides UpdateSummary (posterior-update.ts:1114-1115) but only surfaces via logger.debug (:217), filtered at LOG_LEVEL=info. The all_deterministic skip emits nothing at all. Nothing at INFO says 'this arm received no evidence'.
  - **what must happen:** TWO changes, in this order. (1) repos/light-dispatch-vessel/src/index.ts:727,802 — stop hardcoding resolver_tier; classify from the actual resolver (or omit the field, which fails open to 'all_stochastic' per posterior-update.ts:855-857). This alone restores leaf learning for ~10k executions/72h. (2) repos/light-dispatch-vessel/src/index.ts:895 — call POST /v2/activities/execution-traces/reach with the dispatch's own verdict, so its successes stop being 'ungraded'. COUNTER: an INFO-level `posterior_no_evidence_total{reason=ungraded|all_deterministic|hook_subscriber|no_signature}` emitted from applyOutcomeToPosteriors — four silent abstentions currently share one invisible exit.

### `L1-credit-07` — successYield / computeDeltas — cost_usd + tasks[].output_impulse_ids → the graded α a success is worth

- **Datum:** `{alphaDelta, betaDelta}` — the actual magnitude of the posterior move.
- **Producer:** repos/activity-api/src/lib/posterior-update.ts:280-299 `successYield(trace, refs)`, reached via computeDeltas at :846. Reads `trace.cost_usd` and `tasks.reduce((s,t) => s + (t.output_impulse_ids?.length ?? 0), 0)`. Refs resolved at :271-278 from substrate_tuning_param rows YIELD_FLOOR/YIELD_COST_REF/YIELD_PROD_REF with env→default (0.5/0.02/4) fallback. Fires on every graded (non-ungraded) trace when GRADED_YIELD_SUCCESS !== '0'.
- **Consumer:** Two call sites feed it two DIFFERENT task projections. (a) Insert path: execution-traces.ts:2950 passes `trace.tasks` = the normalizePersistedTask output, which DOES carry `output_impulse_ids` (:218). (b) Late-grading path: execution-traces.ts:4339 selects `trace.tasks AS tasks` from the `execution` row and passes it at :4429 — the same normalized array, round-tripped through the `trace` blob column. Also consumed by classifyTemplateTiers (:858-877) via `resolver_tier`/`resolver`, which decides `skipVariantUpdate`.
- **Value form:** Producer: `y = floor + (1-floor) * (0.5*1/(1+cost/costRef) + 0.5*min(1, outputs/prodRef))`, clamped to [floor,1] — a real number in [0.5,1]. Consumer: α += y, β += (1-y). CRITICAL VALUE-FORM: when `tasks` is null/absent, `outputs = 0` → productivity 0 → yield ≈ floor + 0.5*(1-floor)*costScore, i.e. a systematically SMALLER α than the same execution graded with its tasks present. Absence is silently read as 'produced nothing', not as 'unknown'.
- **VERDICT — agreement `PASS` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `NONE` · load-bearing `medium`**
  - agreement: Field names agree at both call sites. successYield (posterior-update.ts:280-299) reads trace.cost_usd and sums `t.output_impulse_ids?.length`; refs come from substrate_tuning_param YIELD_FLOOR/YIELD_COST_REF/YIELD_PROD_REF with env→default fallback (:271-278). Insert path passes the normalized trace.tasks (execution-traces.ts:2950), which DOES carry output_impulse_ids (:218); the late-grading path selects `trace.tasks AS tasks` from the execution row (:4339) and passes it at :4429 — and I confirmed the blob really is written, `trace: { tasks: trace.tasks, … }` at execution-traces.ts:2581+, so the round-trip is not hollow. The inventory's real point stands and is confirmed: absence and zero are the same value form. `tasks` is stored as `null` when rawPostedTasks was empty (:1930-1932), and a null/absent tasks array yields outputs=0 → productivity 0 → y ≈ floor + 0.5*(1-floor)*costScore, a systematically smaller α than the same execution graded with its tasks present. The code reads 'unknown' as 'produced nothing' rather than abstaining.
  - **probe:** `SELECT count() FROM execution WHERE trace.tasks = NONE OR array::len(trace.tasks) = 0 GROUP ALL` versus total. HEALTHY = the null-tasks population is empty. Then, for a graded window, compare mean per-execution thompson_alpha movement between the null-tasks and non-null-tasks populations (two VPM snapshots joined to the executions in between); HEALTHY = no systematic gap.
  - visibility: The computed yield is never persisted, never logged at INFO, and never returned. The only observable is the resulting α, which is indistinguishable from a genuinely low-quality success. A degraded grade and an honest grade look identical forever.
  - **what must happen:** repos/activity-api/src/lib/posterior-update.ts:290 — distinguish absent from zero: when `trace.tasks` is null/undefined, fall back to the cost-only half (quality = costScore) or abstain from the productivity term, rather than scoring it as zero productivity. Persist the yield: add `graded_yield` to the trace row so a low α can be attributed to cost, to productivity, or to a missing projection.

### `L1-credit-08` — applyOutcomeToPosteriors → enqueueVariantDelta in-process buffer → flushPosteriors UPDATE variant_performance_metrics

- **Datum:** A coalesced Σδ (alpha, beta) for one (variant_id, org_id) hot row — the global posterior move.
- **Producer:** repos/activity-api/src/lib/posterior-update.ts:890-894 (leaf) and :519-538 (chain-credit ancestors) call `enqueueVariantDelta(activityId, orgId, alphaDelta, betaDelta)`. Condition on the leaf: `!skipVariantUpdate && (alphaDelta!==0||betaDelta!==0)`. lib/posterior-aggregator.ts:141-159 folds the delta into an in-process `Map` keyed `"${variantId} ${orgId}"`; returns true (coalescing on) so the synchronous UPDATE is SKIPPED. Flush timer every POSTERIOR_FLUSH_MS (default 250ms, :101) and it is `unref()`'d (:136).
- **Consumer:** repos/activity-api/src/lib/posterior-aggregator.ts:165-243 `flushPosteriors` — SELECTs the row, applies decay, then `UPDATE variant_performance_metrics SET thompson_alpha=$new_alpha, thompson_beta=$new_beta WHERE variant_id = $activity_id AND org_id = $org_id`. :225-241: a ZERO-ROW update does not throw, so the delta is discarded; counted in module-scope `droppedNoRow` and logged at WARN. On a thrown error the residual delta is re-queued (:243-256). Flush on SIGTERM/SIGINT/beforeExit only (:263-274).

[], missing: body.metadata.missing_shapes ?? []})`.

### `L1-credit-11` — composition_chain (walk-step execution ids) → v_paradigm_execution_traces ancestor lookup → cts write under the ancestor's OWN historical signature (e19997f)

- **Datum:** Per-ancestor `(variant_id, signature, signature_version)` resolved from the ancestor's stored trace row, and the decayed (α,β) delta written against it.
- **Producer:** repos/activity-api/src/lib/posterior-update.ts:1125-1142 fires `propagateCreditAlongChain` fire-and-forget when `!ungraded && trace.composition_chain.length > 0`. :665-668 `SELECT execution_id, variant_id, signature, signature_version FROM v_paradigm_execution_traces WHERE execution_id IN $ids` (deliberately NOT org-scoped, :659-664). :699-707 — `ancestorSig = execution.ancestor_signatures?.[id]?.signature ?? meta?.signature ?? null`. The `ancestor_signatures` override has NO populating caller anywhere (applyOutcomeToPosteriors constructs the ExecutionForChainCredit at :1127-1133 without it), so the trace-row fallback landed by e19997f (2026-08-13, "populate ancestor signature so chain credit reaches the conditioned posterior") is the ONLY live path — CONFIRMED static. Then :548-609 `writeAncestorDelta` UPSERTs context_thompson_scores with `template_id = normalizeActivityId(ancestor.variant_id)`, `context_bucket = ancestorSig`. Upstream, the chain content is set by goal-host: index.ts:8257 `chainExecIds` are the EXECUTION IDS OF PRIOR WALK STEPS, pushed at :9356 and passed as `compositionChain` at :9345.
- **Consumer:** Identical to L1-credit-10's consumer: repos/activity-api/src/routes/activities.ts:6188-6206, keyed on a signature derived from the CURRENT pool at selection time. Also read back (unconditioned half) by discover-by-shapes.ts:261 via the parallel VPM write at posterior-update.ts:519-538.
- **Value form:** WRITE key = the 16-hex signature stamped on the ancestor's own trace at ITS execution — a snapshot of an EARLIER pool. READ key = 16-hex over the pool as it stands at a LATER selection. Because the walk's pool grows monotonically (goal-host accumulates `producedShapes`), the ancestor's signature is by construction a strictly smaller shape set than any pool observed after it, so the two sorted-comma-joins differ whenever any shape was produced in between. `ancestorId` value form is bare (normalizeActivityId at :693), same as the leaf.
- **VERDICT — agreement `FAIL` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `NONE` · load-bearing `high`**
  - agreement: The ancestor LOOKUP passes; the write key fails. Confirmed: propagateCreditAlongChain fires fire-and-forget when !ungraded && composition_chain.length > 0 (posterior-update.ts:1125-1142); the batch resolve at :665-668 selects execution_id, variant_id, signature, signature_version from v_paradigm_execution_traces deliberately un-org-scoped (:659-664), and the view really does project all four over `execution` (sql/migrations/167:38-50,79). e19997f STATUS SETTLED BY MY OWN GREP, not by trusting the comment: `grep -rn ancestor_signatures repos/activity-api/src` returns exactly three hits — the type declaration (:174), the read (:699), and the comment (:702). No populating caller exists, so `meta?.signature` from the trace row is the ONLY live source of an ancestor signature. The chain semantics claim also checked directly: goal-host index.ts:8257 seeds chainExecIds from opts.compositionChain, :9356 pushes each executed step's trace.id, and :9345 passes it as compositionChain — so the field documented as a root-first ancestor chain is in fact PRIOR SEQUENTIAL WALK STEPS, and λ^depth credits the most recent prior step most. THE FAILURE: writeAncestorDelta (:548-609) UPSERTs cts with context_bucket = the ancestor's OWN historical pool signature, read at selection against a signature derived from the CURRENT pool. Within one walk that can never match once anything has been produced (goal-host's producedShapes grows monotonically), and across walks it needs a structurally identical repeat at the same position. And it is moot anyway while L1-credit-10 leg (ii) holds — the bare template_id it writes is unreadable by the prefixed read. TWO further asymmetries CONFIRMED: writeAncestorDelta has NO cardinality cap (cf. :983) and NO HOOK_SUBSCRIBER_PATTERN exclusion (cf. :974, pattern defined at :134), so chain credit can create unbounded buckets and can credit hook-subscriber templates the leaf write deliberately excludes.
  - **probe:** `SELECT count() FROM execution WHERE composition_chain != NONE AND array::len(composition_chain) > 0 GROUP ALL` versus total — HEALTHY = the composed-walk fraction, non-trivial. Then pick one such execution, read its ancestors' `signature` values, and check `SELECT template_id, context_bucket, n_observations FROM context_thompson_scores WHERE context_bucket IN [<those signatures>]` — HEALTHY = rows exist at those buckets. Do NOT probe via `chain_credit_no_sig` (posterior-update.ts:731 is logger.debug, filtered at LOG_LEVEL=info).
  - visibility: At INFO the entire chain-credit path is silent. The only instruments are a debug event (:731) and warn-on-throw (:592). A chain that resolves zero ancestors, or resolves them and writes buckets no reader can address, produces no observable difference from one that works.
  - **what must happen:** repos/activity-api/src/lib/posterior-update.ts:548-609 — apply the leaf's two guards to writeAncestorDelta (HOOK_SUBSCRIBER_PATTERN test and SIGNATURE_CARDINALITY_CAP); a write path that can create unbounded buckets while its sibling is capped at 200 is an unmanaged cardinality source. Then the substantive question is a MEASURE-FIRST: whether crediting an ancestor under its own historical signature is the right key at all, or whether the ancestor should be credited under the signature that was current when the CHOICE to use it was made. Measurement: on a local stack, log per chain-credit write both the ancestor's stored signature and the leaf's signature, and count how often either is later derivable at selection. Do not change the key until that number exists.

### `L1-credit-12` — v0 context_bucket posterior — an ingest branch with no producer anywhere in the fleet

- **Datum:** `context_bucket: string` (8 hex) — the v0 (task-semantics + shapes + org) context key, and the cts rows written under it with NO signature_version.

For the bucket ever to be credited, an executor must ECHO it back on the trace.

Condition: `isValidBucket(body.metadata?.context_bucket ?? body.selection_metadata?.context_bucket)` (strict `/^[0-9a-f]{8}$/`) AND `!ctxUngraded`. Read back at activities.ts:6083-6098 (`WHERE ... context_bucket = $bucket AND template_id IN $ids`) feeding `contextScoresMap` and the blendWeight at :6524-6530.
- **Value form:** Producer emits the key inside `selection_metadata`; the ingest accepts it at `body.metadata.context_bucket` or `body.selection_metadata.context_bucket`. NEITHER field is emitted by any fleet trace poster: `grep -rn context_bucket repos/ias-executor-ts/src repos/goal-host-vessel/src repos/light-dispatch-vessel/src` returns nothing on HEAD. The only other writer, POST /selection-feedback (activities.ts:11199-11224), has no caller in any vessel repo. Note also the v0 CREATE at :3105-3125 omits `signature_version` entirely, so v0 rows sit in a different namespace from the v1 read at :6195.
- **VERDICT — agreement `FAIL` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `NEGATIVE` · load-bearing `low`**
  - agreement: CONFIRMED by grep on HEAD — no producer exists. The v0 bucket is computed at activities.ts:5933-5939 via computeContextBucket (session-context.ts:188-202, `digest().slice(0,4)` = 8 hex) and returned inside each recommendation's selection_metadata; the ingest accepts it only at `body.metadata.context_bucket ?? body.selection_metadata.context_bucket` (execution-traces.ts:3066-3068, strict /^[0-9a-f]{8}$/). `grep -rn context_bucket repos/*/src --include=*.ts | grep -v activity-api` returns only obsidian-vessel/src/presentation/presentation-policy.ts:91 (a UI read of a returned field) and a ribosome comment — no trace poster echoes it back. The one other writer, POST /selection-feedback (activities.ts:11199-11224), likewise has no caller: `grep -rn selection-feedback repos/*/src --include=*.ts` returns nothing. I verified both greps myself rather than inheriting them. Additionally the v0 CREATE at :3105-3125 omits signature_version entirely, so v0 rows sit in a different namespace from the v1 read at :6195 — and the v0 read at :6081-6091 binds the same PREFIXED activityIds against bare template_ids, so even legacy rows are unaddressable.

Note the row COUNT alone is not liveness — legacy rows accumulate and are then read by nobody.

  - **what must happen:** DECIDE, then act — do not add a producer by reflex. The v1 signature path supersedes v0, so the honest change is to RETIRE the v0 branch: delete the credit block at execution-traces.ts:3084-3140 and the blend at activities.ts:6524-6530, or (if the blend is still wanted) make ias-executor and light-dispatch echo `metadata.context_bucket` from the recommendation they acted on. Either way, a dead branch that a reader silently treats as 'cold' must not remain. ### `L1-credit-13` — POST /reach late grading — the preRow projection, the idempotence marker, and what it does NOT carry forward

- **Datum:** The late honest-reach verdict converted into a posterior move for a trace the insert path abstained on.
- **Producer:** repos/goal-host-vessel/src/index.ts:972 POSTs to `${ACTIVITY_API_ENDPOINT}/v2/activities/execution-traces/reach` after the walk grades. Fires only for goal-host walks; light-dispatch never calls it.
- **Consumer:** repos/activity-api/src/routes/execution-traces.ts:4308-4470. :4339 pre-read: `SELECT variant_id, activity_id, success, tags, cost_usd, org_id, signature, signature_version, composition_chain, failure_mode, trace.tasks AS tasks FROM type::thing('execution', $execution_id)`. :4406 guard: `preReadOk && preRow && preActivityId && !preIsSatellite && !preTags.includes('reach_graded:true')`, then only when `classifyReach(preTags) === 'ungraded'` (:4412-4414). :4416-4423 writes the `reach_graded:true` marker BEFORE crediting. :4426-4445 calls applyOutcomeToPosteriors with `tags = [...preTags, 'reached:true'|'reached:false']`.
- **Value form:** The pre-read is an EXPLICIT PROJECTION and a silent dropper: it does NOT select `input_impulse_shapes`, `output_impulse_shapes`, `metadata`, `sibling_group_size` or `input_impulse_shapes` for the D2.3 embed. Consequences, all CONFIRMED by reading: (a) the cts write reuses the STORED `signature` — correct, no re-derivation drift; but if the insert never stamped one (empty sigShapes), the conditional write is skipped forever with no second chance; (b) `sibling_group_size` is absent, so a horizontally-composed sibling credited late inflates every shared ancestor k-fold (the exact k-division the field exists for, posterior-update.ts:652); (c) `input_impulse_shapes` is absent, so the signature-embedding hop (:1086-1093) never fires on the late path; (d) `trace.tasks` reached through the blob column feeds successYield and classifyTemplateTiers — an empty blob silently lowers α (see L1-credit-07).
- **VERDICT — agreement `FAIL` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `PASS` · load-bearing `medium`**
  - agreement: PASS on the fields the pre-read selects; FAIL by omission on two. The projection at execution-traces.ts:4339 is `SELECT variant_id, activity_id, success, tags, cost_usd, org_id, signature, signature_version, composition_chain, failure_mode, trace.tasks AS tasks` — an explicit projection and therefore a silent dropper. It omits sibling_group_size, so a horizontally-composed sibling credited late inflates every shared ancestor k-fold — exactly the k-division the field exists for (posterior-update.ts:652 `siblingDivisor`); and it omits input_impulse_shapes, so the signature-embedding hop (:1086-1093) never fires on the late path. Confirmed in the other direction, and worth keeping: the pre-read plus the `reach_graded:true` marker written BEFORE the credit (:4416-4423) is a genuinely well-built double-count guard, the guard chain at :4406-4414 is complete (preReadOk, preRow, preActivityId, not-satellite, not-already-graded, verdict==='ungraded'), and reusing the STORED signature avoids re-derivation drift — at the cost that a trace whose insert never stamped one gets no second chance. The `trace.tasks` blob round-trip is real, not hollow: paradigmExecution writes `trace: { tasks: trace.tasks, … }` at :2581+. goal-host's caller is correspondingly careful, checking `j.updated` rather than r.ok (index.ts:984-986) because 200 {"updated":0} would otherwise read as success.
  - **probe:** Journal at INFO (safe — both branches log at INFO): count `[reach-patch] late reach verdict graded into posteriors` (execution-traces.ts:4447) versus `[reach-patch] posterior grading skipped` (:4453) over a window with walks. HEALTHY = graded ≈ walk count, skipped ≈ 0. Independently `SELECT count() FROM execution WHERE tags CONTAINS 'reach_graded:true' GROUP ALL` versus the goal-host walk count over the same window.
  - visibility: The best-instrumented hop in this stretch. Both outcomes log at INFO with the execution id and the pre-verdict, the satellite skip logs separately (:4459), the marker-write failure logs, and the caller refuses to read HTTP 200 as success. This is the standard the other fourteen seams should be measured against.
  - **what must happen:** repos/activity-api/src/routes/execution-traces.ts:4339 — add `metadata.siblingGroupSize AS sibling_group_size` and `input_impulse_shapes` to the pre-read projection, and thread sibling_group_size into the applyOutcomeToPosteriors call at :4426-4445. Without it, late-graded horizontal compositions inflate shared ancestors by the fan-out width. ### `L1-credit-14` — updateShapeActivityScores → impulse_shape_activity_score (a write-only table) while the real reader is a view over `execution`

- **Datum:** Per-(org, shape, activity) success/failure counters, intended as the shape-conditioned Thompson prior.
- **Producer:** repos/activity-api/src/routes/execution-traces.ts:3402-3424 — `inputShapes = body.input_impulse_shapes || trace.input_impulse_shapes || metadata.inputShapes || metadata.input_shapes || []`; fires fire-and-forget when `inputShapes.length > 0 && trace.variant_id && traceOrgId`. Calls repos/activity-api/src/db/paradigm.ts:1743-1790, which UPSERTs ONE ROW PER SHAPE: `UPSERT impulse_shape_activity_score:[$org_id, $shape, $activity_id] MERGE {...}`. A second writer at routes/activities.scoring.ts:173.
- **Consumer:** NONE for selection. `grep -rn impulse_shape_activity_score src/ --include=*.ts` returns only the UPSERT statements themselves (paradigm.ts:1766-1783, activities.scoring.ts:173-184) and a migration table list (cli/migrate-org-to-account.ts:24) — there is no SELECT anywhere outside the UPSERT's own sub-expressions. The shape-conditioned scores actually consumed at selection come from paradigm.ts:1039-1090 `getShapeConditionedScores`, which queries `v_shape_conditioned_score` — a VIEW OVER `execution` (sql/schemas/023-shape-conditioned-scores.surql:19-47) grouping by `array::sort(array::distinct(input_impulse_shapes))`, gated `WHERE input_impulse_shapes IS NOT NONE AND array::len(input_impulse_shapes) > 0`.
- **Value form:** WRITE: N rows, one per individual shape, keyed by a composite record id `[org_id, shape, activity_id]`. READ: one row per SHAPE-SET, keyed by `shape_signature = computeShapeSignature(inputShapes)` = `[...new Set(shapes)].sort()` — a sorted ARRAY, not a hash, compared with `=`. Two incompatible granularities (per-shape vs per-set) in addition to two different tables. Second value-form question: the view is filtered `org_id = $org_id` with `fullOrgId = orgId.startsWith('organizations:') ? orgId : 'organizations:'+orgId` (paradigm.ts:1063), while `execution.org_id` is written from `traceOrgId = body.org_id || jwtAuth?.orgId || session?.org_id || 'public'` (execution-traces.ts:1849) — a bare 'public' on the default path.
- **VERDICT — agreement `FAIL` · liveness `UNVERIFIED` · visibility `NEGATIVE` · load-bearing `medium`**
  - agreement: CONFIRMED, and worse than the inventory states. (a) THE WRITE TARGET HAS NO READER: `grep -rn impulse_shape_activity_score src/ sql/` returns only UPSERT statements (paradigm.ts:1766-1783, activities.scoring.ts:173-190), their own nested read-modify-write subqueries, schema DEFINEs, migrations and tests. The repo already knows: routes/coalesce-precedence.test.ts:98-113 is a test literally titled 'the orphan is pinned, not assumed', documenting that it has no reader as of 2026-08-17. (b) GRANULARITY: the write is one row per individual shape keyed `[org_id, shape, activity_id]`; the live reader groups by shape-SET (`array::sort(array::distinct(input_impulse_shapes))`, sql/schemas/023:24) matched against computeShapeSignature = a sorted array (paradigm.ts:1017-1019). Incompatible even if a reader were added. (c) THE LIVE READER IS TRIPLE-KILLED — my correction. getShapeConditionedScores queries v_shape_conditioned_score, and: (1) the view is gated `WHERE input_impulse_shapes IS NOT NONE AND array::len(...) > 0` (sql/schemas/023:39-40), which light-dispatch never satisfies (L1-credit-02); (2) paradigm.ts:1063 binds org_id ONLY as `organizations:<x>` while execution rows store what the ingest computed — bare 'public' on the default path (execution-traces.ts:1849, stored verbatim by paradigm.ts:436-437); (3) paradigm.ts:1073 binds the PREFIXED activityIds against the view's bare `execution.activity_id`. getActivityScores at paradigm.ts:581-598 — forty lines above — handles BOTH org forms AND normalizes the ids. Leg (2)'s reach beyond the default path is UNVERIFIED: whether authenticated posters supply a prefixed org form depends on data.

### `L1-credit-15` — The four abstention gates on the write side vs the two admission gates on the read side

- **Datum:** The boolean decisions that suppress a posterior write, and the independent thresholds that suppress its use.
- **Producer:** WRITE-SIDE abstentions, all in repos/activity-api/src/lib/posterior-update.ts: (1) `ungraded` → `{0,0}` (:842-846, from classifyReach — see L1-credit-06); (2) `skipVariantUpdate = classifyTemplateTiers(...) === 'all_deterministic'` (:858-878) suppresses BOTH the VPM update and the v1 cts write, while chain credit still fires (:1125); (3) `HOOK_SUBSCRIBER_PATTERN = /validator-dispatch|slot-binding|create-shape-provider-goal/` tested against the activityId (:134, :974) — leaf cts only, NOT applied to writeAncestorDelta; (4) `SIGNATURE_CARDINALITY_CAP` (:983, env, default 200) blocks the CREATE branch once a template holds 200 buckets — again leaf-only.
- **Consumer:** READ-SIDE admission, repos/activity-api/src/routes/activities.ts:6543-6551: `leafConditionalActive = sigRow && sigRow.n_observations >= SIGNATURE_SAMPLING_FLOOR (5) && validTemplates.length >= 2`. Below that floor the conditional posterior is computed, matched, and then NOT USED; the D5 cluster fallback at :6560+ engages only for a cold leaf.
- **Value form:** Producer gates are booleans/regex/int-env, evaluated per trace, none of them persisted on the row. Consumer gate is an integer comparison against a stored `n_observations`. Note `n_observations` increments by exactly 1 per write regardless of the fractional (α,β) magnitude (posterior-update.ts:1016), so it counts OBSERVATIONS, not evidence weight — an arm credited 5× at yield 0.5 crosses the same floor as one credited 5× at yield 1.0. Note also the tier classifier fails OPEN: a trace with no `resolver_tier` on any task returns 'all_stochastic' (:855-857), so the M4 skip only fires when tiers are explicitly present.
- **VERDICT — agreement `FAIL` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `NONE` · load-bearing `high`**
  - agreement: Each gate agrees with itself; the composition does not, and it is worse than 'unstated'. WRITE-SIDE, all confirmed in posterior-update.ts: (1) ungraded → {0,0} (:842-846); (2) skipVariantUpdate = classifyTemplateTiers === 'all_deterministic' (:858-878) suppresses BOTH the VPM update and the v1 cts write while chain credit still fires (:1125); (3) HOOK_SUBSCRIBER_PATTERN (:134, tested at :974) — leaf cts only, NOT applied to writeAncestorDelta; (4) SIGNATURE_CARDINALITY_CAP (:983, default 200) blocks the CREATE branch only — also leaf-only. READ-SIDE: leafConditionalActive requires sigRow && n_observations ≥ SIGNATURE_SAMPLING_FLOOR (5, activities.ts:6111) && validTemplates.length ≥ 2 (:6543-6551). Two things the inventory understates. FIRST: given L1-credit-10 leg (ii), sigScoresMap is ALWAYS empty, so `sigRow` is always undefined and leafConditionalActive is PROVABLY FALSE on HEAD — the joint condition is not merely undocumented, it is currently unsatisfiable, and no amount of write-side traffic can change that. SECOND: n_observations increments by exactly 1 per write regardless of the fractional (α,β) magnitude (:1016), so the floor counts OBSERVATIONS, not evidence weight — five yield-0.5 credits clear the same bar as five yield-1.0 credits. Also confirmed: the tier classifier fails OPEN (tier-classifier.ts:80-83 → 'all_stochastic' when tasks is empty), so gate (2) only bites when resolver_tier is explicitly present — which, per L1-credit-06, light-dispatch always makes true.
  - **probe:** Two queries on the local stack. (1) `SELECT template_id, count() AS buckets FROM context_thompson_scores WHERE signature_version = 1 GROUP BY template_id ORDER BY buckets DESC LIMIT 10` — HEALTHY = no template pinned at exactly 200 (the cap); a template sitting at the cap has stopped learning new contexts silently. (2) `SELECT count() FROM context_thompson_scores WHERE signature_version = 1 AND n_observations >= 5 GROUP ALL` versus the total v1 row count — HEALTHY = a meaningful fraction; near-zero means every row is below the admission floor and key agreement would not help even if it existed.
  - visibility: Gates 2, 3 and 4 are entirely silent: `skipped_reason:'all_deterministic'` rides only the debug UpdateSummary (posterior-update.ts:217, filtered at LOG_LEVEL=info), and the cardinality cap and hook exclusion emit nothing at all. Four independent ways for an execution to teach nothing, none of them countable.
  - **what must happen:** repos/activity-api/src/lib/posterior-update.ts — emit ONE counter with a reason label at every abstention: `posterior_no_evidence_total{reason=ungraded|all_deterministic|hook_subscriber|cardinality_cap|no_signature}`, at INFO, from the four gates above. That single counter turns five invisible suppressors into a readable budget and is the prerequisite for judging any of them. Then state the joint condition in one place (a comment or a predicate function) so the write-side and read-side gates stop being added independently. NOTE the ordering constraint: none of this is measurable until L1-credit-10's id-form fix lands, because the read-side floor is currently unreachable by construction.

**Stretch summary.** Validated all 15 L1-credit seams statically at HEAD (activity-api b984e34, ias-executor ff3df57, goal-host b5e4b56, light-dispatch fb662d0). The inventory is substantially accurate; every load-bearing claim I re-checked held. Four adversarial corrections, all CONFIRMED by reading, change the picture.

CORRECTION 1 — THE REAL HEADLINE IS THE ID FORM, NOT THE SIGNATURE. The inventory ranks the signature pre-image divergence first and defers template_id to R2-select-11. That order is wrong. All three context_thompson_scores WRITERS store a BARE template_id: posterior-update.ts:825 and :693 both apply normalizeActivityId (paradigm.ts:1932-1939, which strips `^activity:` and ⟨⟩), and the v0 writer binds `template_id: trace.variant_id` = the poster's raw bare template id (execution-traces.ts:3131). All three cts READS in /recommend bind the PREFIXED form: activities.ts:6013-6015 builds `activityIds = templates.map(t => normalizeRecordId(t.id || t.variant_id))`, and normalizeRecordId (utils/surrealdb-types.ts:52-69) is a passthrough — it stringifies a SurrealDB RecordId to `activity:⟨…⟩` and strips nothing. Those prefixed ids are bound as `$ids` at :6091 (v0), :6199 (v1) and :6216 (repair v2), and the blend loop then looks the row up with the same prefixed key at :6399/:6528/:6543. `template_id IN $ids` therefore matches ZERO rows, structurally, regardless of signature. The decisive in-file contrast: getActivityScores forty lines away in paradigm.ts:596-598 DOES normalize (`id.replace(/^activity:/,'').replace(/[⟨⟩`]/g,'')`) before its IN-list — the same array, the same request, one query defended and three not. Consequence: `leafConditionalActive` (activities.ts:6543-6551) is provably false on HEAD because sigScoresMap is always empty, so L1-credit-15's joint condition is not merely unstated — it is unsatisfiable. The signature pre-image divergence (L1-credit-10 leg iii) is real and independent, but it is second in line.

CORRECTION 2 — LIGHT-DISPATCH MOVES NO POSTERIOR ANYWHERE, NOT "β ON FAILURES". light-dispatch hardcodes `resolver_tier: "deterministic"` on EVERY task record at index.ts:727 and :802, irrespective of the actual resolver. posterior-update.ts:858-877 maps that to `{resolver:'bash'}`, classifyTemplateTiers (tier-classifier.ts:96) returns 'all_deterministic', and `skipVariantUpdate` then suppresses BOTH the leaf variant_performance_metrics update (:890) and the v1 cts write (:972). So the inventory's L1-credit-06 asymmetry ("a failure penalizes β, a success moves nothing") is too generous: at the leaf, a light-dispatch FAILURE also moves nothing. The only surviving channel is chain credit (:1125, gated on !ungraded), which is blame-only for this producer — and the ingest comment at execution-traces.ts:1911-1917 puts light-dispatch at ~10,166 ROOT executions per 72h, i.e. an empty composition_chain. Net: the fleet's highest-volume producer teaches the bandit nothing, in either direction.

CORRECTION 3 — posteriorDeltasDroppedNoRow() HAS NO READER. `grep -rn posteriorDeltasDroppedNoRow src/` returns only its own definition (posterior-aggregator.ts:120-122). Its docstring claims it is "Exposed for the health route and for tests"; that sentence is false. L1-credit-08 visibility drops from the inventory's PASS to WEAK (a WARN line at :230, no counted surface).

CORRECTION 4 — getShapeConditionedScores is killed THREE independent ways, not one. Beyond the empty-input_impulse_shapes starvation the inventory names: paradigm.ts:1063 compares `org_id = $org_id` with ONLY the `organizations:`-prefixed form, while execution rows carry whatever the ingest computed — bare 'public' on the default path (execution-traces.ts:1849, stored verbatim by paradigm.ts:436-437); and :1073 passes the same un-normalized PREFIXED activityIds against `v_shape_conditioned_score.activity_id`, which projects the bare `execution.activity_id`. getActivityScores, forty lines up, handles both org forms AND normalizes the ids.

Sub-claims I independently settled rather than inheriting: `ancestor_signatures` has no populating caller (only posterior-update.ts:174 decl and :699 read); POST /selection-feedback has no caller in any vessel repo; goal-host's composition_chain really is prior sequential walk-step execution ids (index.ts:8257 chainExecIds, pushed at :9356, passed at :9345). Method note carried forward: LOG_LEVEL defaults to info, so no probe below greps a logger.debug line.

# Addendum — validations for R1-intake and L2-structure


## RECALL 1 — goal intake to target shapes


All 14 enumerated seams exist and both sides were opened independently. Line citations in the inventory are accurate against HEAD (goal-host-vessel b5e4b56); goal-target-inference.ts is byte-for-byte where the inventory says it is, and index.ts drifted by at most 7 lines (producer of intake-01 is 14930-14951, not 14937-14950). AGREEMENT is settled statically for all 14: 7 FAIL, 7 PASS. Two claims were escalated from "suspected" to CONFIRMED by running the module locally in a unit harness (no network, no credentials — /tmp/probe.test.ts, 4/4 pass): (a) infer-08, a composition-ask goal with the LLM declining returns {shapes:["source_code","memoryNote_write"],confidence:0.8} with decisionCache size 0, and the env-gate route returns confidence 0.7 with cache size 0 — so `inference_confidence` persists as null exactly where the decision was most confident; (b) infer-09, "Run the test suite for repos/goal-host-vessel and record the pass count in a memory note" returns ["test_suite"] with a throwing LLM stub, i.e. the write clause is dropped and the LLM is never consulted. THREE ENUMERATION CORRECTIONS. (1) infer-07's stated mechanism is wrong: I verified the producer's sorted-join dedupe (goal-target-inference.ts:671,683) already drops any set-equal alternative — my probe returned alternatives:[] for a permutation — so consumer (a) can never receive a permutation of the PRIMARY. The reachable defect is that consumer (a) compares positionally against the POST-OVERRIDE seededOutputShapes (reordered by split-12 at :11112 and widened by override-11 at :10994), so a permutation of the CURRENT set does survive; and the unambiguous failure in this seam is `terminalOutputShapes: undefined` at :11489. (2) infer-10's cross-namespace-eviction hazard is NOT reachable in production: namespace (a) is written only by inferGoalTargetShapes, which has zero call sites in src/ (grep: index.ts:246 imports it, nothing invokes it; 19 references in test/ keep it green). (3) infer-06 and infer-08 are correctly two seams, not one (return value vs Map write, disjoint consumers); infer-10 and split-12 overlap on the cache-hit order loss but the datum differs (shared Map vs DerivationSplit return), so it is a cross-reference, not a double count. TWO LOUD NEGATIVES. GET /health at index.ts:15546-15549 returns an unconditional status:"healthy" — every seam in this stretch can be dead while goal-host reports green, which disables every watchdog above it. And infer-08/infer-09 are each concealed by a PASSING TEST: test/goal-target-inference.test.ts:355-382 asserts "shortcut decisions populate the decision cache" and passes because it only exercises the seven remember() exits, never the four deterministic decisions carried in the `empty` value; test/goal-target-inference.test.ts:233-255 asserts the composition guard and passes only because its vocabulary lacks `test_suite`, so no COMPOSITIONS goal ever reaches the _namedShape route above the guard. A green suite over a partially-closed class is what let both survive. NO LIVENESS IS ASSERTED ANYWHERE: no production access was used, no credential was read, no authenticated call was made. Every liveness verdict is UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK with a probe written to run against a locally booted unmasked fleet (goal-host :18210, discovery :18100, activity-api :18080).


### `R1-intake-01` — agreement `FAIL` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `NONE` · load-bearing `high`

- agreement: CONFIRMED explicit-projection dropper. Producer is index.ts:14930-14951 (inventory said 14937-14950 — 7-line drift, same block). The synthetic Request body names exactly 8 keys: goal, variables, tags, expected_output_shapes, operator, targetTemplateId, parent_execution_id, composition_chain. The consumer handleRunGoal reads strictly more: ablation (index.ts:14087-14089), learning_mode (index.ts:14091-14094), loaded_concept_ids (index.ts:14105-14114), and requeue_dispatch_id which survives only because it rides inside `variables`. The synthetic Request also sets only Content-Type, so x-caller-vessel is absent and the D3 cross-vessel guard at index.ts:14123-14130 is structurally unreachable for this route. Consequence: a federated caller asking for learning_mode:'observe' gets a LEARNING dispatch (goal-paths, reached-command cache and mints all written), and an ablation arm silently runs as the normal arm. Seam exists exactly as described; producer/consumer correctly identified.
- **probe:** On a locally booted unmasked stack: `curl -s localhost:18210/resolve -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"type":"goal_dispatch_async","goal":"probe-nonce-A count files in repos/goal-host-vessel/src","learning_mode":"observe","ablation":{"forceFloor":true}}'`, take the returned dispatchId, poll `curl -s localhost:18210/executions/<dispatchId>` to terminal, then read the trace tags via activity-api. HEALTHY = the tag list contains BOTH `learning_mode:observe` and an `ablation:` tag (they are pushed at index.ts:14621-14622 only when the flags parsed). Today's predicted count is 0 of 1. Control: the same dispatch sent directly to `POST localhost:18210/run-goal` with the identical body MUST show both tags — if the control also shows 0, the defect is downstream of this seam, not in the projection.
- visibility: No log line anywhere names the dropped keys; the dispatch returns a normal 202 with a dispatchId. No test in repos/goal-host-vessel/test covers goalDispatchAsync or learning_mode (grep over test/ returns zero files). Compounded by the unconditional GET /health at index.ts:15546-15549.
- **what must happen:** Stop rebuilding the body key-by-key. Forward the parsed body wholesale (spread the original object, overriding only `goal` with the string-normalised value) and copy the inbound headers onto the synthetic Request so the D3 guard can see x-caller-vessel. Then add a class detector rather than patching the instance: a test that asserts every key handleRunGoal reads is present in the ingress projection, driven by the parse list itself, so the next key added to handleRunGoal fails the test instead of silently vanishing over the ingress.

### `R1-intake-02` — agreement `FAIL` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `NEGATIVE` · load-bearing `critical`

- agreement: CONFIRMED agreement failure by OMISSION, and the type system permits it because every dropped key is optional. The sync /resolve parse at index.ts:15064-15087 extracts only goal, target_template_id, variables, parent_execution_id, composition_chain. The call at index.ts:15097-15105 passes an opts literal with no operator, tags, expectedOutputShapes, stepSink, learningSink, ablation or learningMode — yet runGoalWithRecovery's own opts type DECLARES operatorOrigin (index.ts:10611), stepSink/learningSink (index.ts:10623-10625) and ablation/learningMode (index.ts:10627-10629). So the consumer is capable of reading all seven and receives undefined for all seven. Downstream: operatorOrigin defaults false and is read as `opts.operatorOrigin === true` at index.ts:11212 and index.ts:11722, so an operator dispatching synchronously is graded AUTONOMOUS for the feature_compose slot reservation; the `operator:<id>` trace tag is minted only on the /run-goal path (index.ts:14546-14547) and therefore never on /resolve, breaking CLAUDE.md's attributability contract for the synchronous surface; and a caller-declared expected_output_shapes is silently discarded and re-inferred.
- **probe:** On a local stack: `curl -s localhost:18210/resolve -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"type":"goal_execution","goal":"probe-nonce-B report the name field of repos/goal-host-vessel/package.json","operator":"probe-op-1","expected_output_shapes":["shellResult"]}'`. Take the returned executionId and read the trace's tags from activity-api. HEALTHY = tags contain `operator:probe-op-1` — i.e. count of traces bearing that tag = 1. Today's predicted value is 0. Second reading on the same dispatch: the goal-host journal line `[goal-host-vessel] /resolve: goal-target inference` MUST NOT appear, because a caller-declared expected_output_shapes should skip inference (index.ts:10676); if it appears, the declared shapes were discarded. Control: the identical body POSTed to /run-goal yields the tag, proving the vessel can emit it at all.
- visibility: LOUD NEGATIVE. The response at index.ts:15110-15120 returns `resolved: true` with executionId, status, selectedTemplateId, completionShapes and a goalReachReason — a fully normal-looking success body — while operator identity, caller tags and the caller's declared target contract were all discarded. A caller has no field to inspect that would differ. Layered on top of the unconditional /health at index.ts:15546-15549.
- **what must happen:** Parse operator, tags, expected_output_shapes, ablation and learning_mode at index.ts:15064-15087 and thread them into the opts literal at index.ts:15097, deriving operatorOrigin the same way /run-goal does. The class fix is to stop having two hand-maintained intake parsers: extract one `parseDispatchBody(req)` used by /run-goal, the ingress projection (intake-01) and /resolve, so a key added once is read by all three. Until then, the honest interim is for /resolve to REFUSE (400) a body carrying operator/tags/expected_output_shapes it will not honour, rather than answering resolved:true.

### `R1-intake-03` — agreement `FAIL` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `WEAK` · load-bearing `medium`

- agreement: CONFIRMED two incompatible definitions of 'the same goal' in one process. The coalescing consumer at index.ts:14142-14150 keys on `goal.trim()` — raw, case-sensitive, whitespace-run-sensitive, line-wrap-sensitive — scanning executionStore for a running record whose `rec.goal.trim()` matches. The record it scans stores the goal VERBATIM (index.ts:14205). The system's other identity for the same goal, goalHashOf (goal-target-inference.ts:39-45), normalises NFC → lowercase → collapse whitespace → trim before FNV-1a, and that hash keys inferredTargetDecisionCache, reachedCommandCache, the lexical-rebind donor key and flushRouterFeedback. Direction of divergence: coalescing UNDER-fires. Two dispatches differing only in case or line-wrapping run as separate concurrent walks (defeating the livelock bound the comment at index.ts:14136-14141 says this exists for) while SHARING every goalHash-keyed cache — so dispatch B can be served dispatch A's cached inference and cached reached-command while being recorded as an independent execution. Seam and both sides correctly identified.
- **probe:** On a local stack, fire two dispatches within the same second whose text differs only in case: `curl -s localhost:18210/run-goal -d '{"goal":"Count the TypeScript files under repos/goal-host-vessel/src"}'` and immediately `curl -s localhost:18210/run-goal -d '{"goal":"count the typescript files under repos/goal-host-vessel/src"}'`. HEALTHY = the second response body contains `"coalesced":true` and the SAME dispatchId as the first (count of distinct dispatchIds = 1). Today's predicted result is 2 distinct dispatchIds and no coalesced flag. Positive control for the probe itself: repeat with byte-identical text — that MUST return coalesced:true, proving the loop runs at all and the negative above is a normalisation miss, not a dead loop.
- visibility: A coalescing HIT logs `[run-goal] coalesced duplicate in-flight goal` at index.ts:14148 — but a MISS logs nothing, and a miss is indistinguishable from 'there was genuinely no duplicate'. There is also no counter, so the miss rate is unmeasurable. Secondary confound the inventory correctly flags: pruneStore() at index.ts:14152 runs immediately after, so a miss can additionally be a pruning artefact. No test covers coalescing (grep for 'coalesced' over test/ returns nothing).
- **what must happen:** Key the coalescing loop on goalHashOf(goal), not goal.trim(), so the in-flight identity and the cache identity are the SAME identity — and store that hash on DispatchRecord so the scan is a map lookup rather than a linear scan. Detector for the class: an assertion that any two goals sharing a goalHash also coalesce, driven by the same normalisation function, so the two identities cannot drift apart again.

### `R1-intake-04` — agreement `PASS` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `PASS` · load-bearing `high`

- agreement: Seam exists as described and both sides agree on key, type and value form. The trigger IIFE at index.ts:14166-14204 is precedence-ordered exactly as enumerated (operator first at index.ts:14180, 'run-goal' floor at index.ts:14203), written to the record at index.ts:14205, and read as `operatorOrigin: trigger === "operator"` at index.ts:14599, consumed as `opts.operatorOrigin === true` at index.ts:11212 and index.ts:11722. Bare string on both sides for trigger; `operator:<id>` with a colon for the tag (index.ts:14546-14547); boolean defaulting FALSE for operatorOrigin, which is fail-safe and documented at index.ts:10605-10609. TWO HAZARDS THAT ARE NOT AGREEMENT FAILURES BUT MUST BE RECORDED. (1) The classifier reads body.operator, so `operator:<id>` is trace ATTRIBUTION, never origin — any caller can claim operator origin and take the reserved compose slot. (2) effectiveTags is built at index.ts:14547, handed BY REFERENCE to the walk at index.ts:14586, and then MUTATED after the walk at index.ts:14620-14622 (execution_path:, walk_tier:, attempt_count:, learning_mode:, ablation:) — so the array the walk observed and the array a later reader observes are the same object with different contents. The push happens after the walk awaits, so the walk itself is unaffected, but any trace write still in flight, or any consumer holding the reference, sees a different tag set than the walk did.
- **probe:** On a local stack, dispatch one goal per trigger class and count the classifier's agreement at the consuming side: `curl -s localhost:18210/run-goal -d '{"goal":"probe-nonce-C ...","operator":"probe-op-2"}'` then `curl -s localhost:18210/resolve -d '{"type":"activeDispatches"}'` and read the record's `trigger` field. HEALTHY = trigger == "operator" for that dispatchId (1 of 1), and the resulting trace carries the tag `operator:probe-op-2`. Then repeat with `{"goal":"...","tags":["gap_generated"]}` — HEALTHY = trigger == "gap-closing" and operatorOrigin false. Fleet-level number that means healthy: over an hour of autonomous traffic, the fraction of dispatch records with trigger == "run-goal" (the unattributable floor at index.ts:14203) should be near 0 — a large share there means the match keys have drifted from what the real dispatchers emit.
- visibility: trigger is serialized onto the DispatchRecord at index.ts:14205 and surfaced through the activeDispatches shape at index.ts:14964 and goalWalkState at index.ts:14983 — i.e. counted at a consumer, not merely logged. The unattributable case is explicitly named rather than serialized as null. The aliasing in hazard (2) is invisible, however: nothing records which snapshot of effectiveTags a given reader saw.
- **what must happen:** Nothing on agreement. Two hardening items: copy effectiveTags before handing it to the walk at index.ts:14586 (or freeze it and build a separate post-walk array) so the tag set a reader sees is the tag set that was in force; and stop treating a self-declared body.operator as sufficient for the reserved compose slot — bind operatorOrigin to an authenticated caller identity, since today the reservation at index.ts:11212 is claimable by any dispatcher that sets a string.

### `R1-vocab-05` — agreement `PASS` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `WEAK` · load-bearing `critical`

- agreement: Seam exists as described; three producers unioned into one deduped string[] at index.ts:4808-4834, consumed as a plain array with exact Set membership at goal-target-inference.ts:562 and goal-target-inference.ts:641, and interpolated verbatim into the LLM prompt at goal-target-inference.ts:603. No normalisation on either side, so colon-, camel- and snake-cased names (obsidian:write_note, memoryNote_write, env_gate_scan) must match byte-exactly — they do, because both sides use the same raw strings. THE FAILURE MODE IS NOT AGREEMENT, IT IS SILENT NARROWING, and I confirmed the asymmetry the inventory names: fetchLearnedDeliverableShapes distinguishes a failed lookup from an empty answer and SAYS WHICH (index.ts:4794 and index.ts:4802-4803), but the registry leg does not — `if (!r.ok) return knownShapesCache?.shapes ?? []` at index.ts:4818 and the bare `catch { return knownShapesCache?.shapes ?? [] }` at index.ts:4836 both return a shorter array with no log. Every deterministic shortcut is guarded by `knownShapes.includes(X)` (goal-target-inference.ts:275, :311, :238-240, :459-461), so a partial vocabulary makes the router quietly DECLINE to route and fall through — the plausible-looking zero. One consumer does fail closed and says so: fileCapabilityGap at index.ts:4923-4927. (2) Consumer side: there is currently NO consumer-side count, which is itself the finding; the runnable substitute is to dispatch one goal and grep the goal-host journal for the inference tap `goal-target inference` at index.ts:10961-10966 and confirm a non-empty inferred_target_shapes, then count failure lines: `docker exec substrate-live journalctl -u goal-host-vessel --since '1 hour ago' | grep -c 'deliverable-shapes] lookup'`. HEALTHY = 0 lookup lines (every lookup succeeded) AND N > 300 for a full local fleet. A non-zero count on the deliverable leg with no corresponding signal on the registry leg means the registry leg is degrading invisibly. The definitive probe requires the instrument named under what_must_happen.
- visibility: Log-only, and asymmetric. The learned-deliverable leg logs both the !r.ok case and the throw case, naming whether a stale cache or an EMPTY vocabulary is being served (index.ts:4794, :4802). The registry leg (index.ts:4818, :4836) and the peer leg (`continue` on !r.ok at index.ts:4759, silent `catch {}` at index.ts:4762) are entirely silent. Nothing counts knownShapes.length at the point of use, so a vocabulary that halved is indistinguishable from a substrate that genuinely serves fewer shapes.
- **what must happen:** Add knownShapes.length (and the per-leg contribution: local / peer / learned) to the inference tap at index.ts:10963-10966, so every dispatch records the size of the vocabulary its decision was made against — that turns a silent narrowing into a countable series at the consuming side. Then apply the failed-lookup-is-not-an-empty-answer treatment already present at index.ts:4794 to the registry leg at index.ts:4818/:4836 and the peer leg at index.ts:4759, so a degraded fetch says which of the two it was.

### `R1-infer-07` — agreement `FAIL` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `WEAK` · load-bearing `high`

- agreement: Seam exists, two consumers correctly identified, BUT THE INVENTORY'S STATED MECHANISM IS WRONG AND I AM CORRECTING IT. The inventory claims the producer treats [A,B] and [B,A] as one framing while consumer (a) treats them as different, so a reframe to a permutation is possible. I ran it: with the LLM returning primary [shellResult, memoryNote_write] and an alternative [memoryNote_write, shellResult], the returned decision had `alternatives: []` — the sorted-join dedupe at goal-target-inference.ts:671 and :683 drops any set-equal alternative before it ever leaves the producer. So consumer (a) can NEVER receive a permutation of the PRIMARY. The defect is real but reachable by a different path: consumer (a) at index.ts:11472-11480 compares positionally against `seededOutputShapes`, which by then may have been REORDERED by the derivation split (index.ts:11112, `[...split.intermediate, ...terminalOutputShapes]`) or WIDENED by the countable union (index.ts:10993). An alternative that legitimately survived the producer's dedupe against the primary can be a permutation of the post-override CURRENT set, and consumer (a) will 'reframe' to it and re-walk the same shapes. THE UNAMBIGUOUS FAILURE IN THIS SEAM IS SEPARATE AND CONFIRMED: index.ts:11489 passes `terminalOutputShapes: undefined`, so a derivation goal that reframes LOSES its derive→emit deferral and the terminal can be satisfied directly from goal text — hollow-note by construction, the exact truncation the split exists to prevent. The same call also drops preferPathway and preferPathwayOrigin, which the primary walk at index.ts:11393-11394 and the suppressed-satisfier retry at index.ts:11450-11451 both pass. Consumer (b) at index.ts:12224-12233 reassigns seededOutputShapes without updating goalTargetDecision.shapes, so the confidence handed to escalateNoProducerToInvestigation at index.ts:12237 describes a set no longer in use — confirmed.
- **probe:** On a local stack, dispatch a derivation goal engineered to go hollow on the first walk, e.g. `curl -s localhost:18210/run-goal -d '{"goal":"probe-nonce-D analyze repos/goal-host-vessel/src/registry-field.ts for code-quality risks and write the findings to a memory note"}'`, then `docker exec substrate-live journalctl -u goal-host-vessel | grep 're-framing to alternative target shapes'` (emitted at index.ts:11481). For each such line, read the FOLLOWING walk's terminal handling. HEALTHY = every reframed walk that includes a *_write shape also logs the deferral, i.e. the count of reframed walks whose terminal write was satisfied WITHOUT a produced intermediate = 0. Frequency reading: count reframe lines per 100 dispatches — if that count is 0 across a mixed workload, consumer (a) is dead (alternatives are always empty, e.g. because the shell-safety-net or producer-lookup override at index.ts:11021/:11032 blanked them) and the seam is a plausible-looking zero rather than a healthy one. Second probe for consumer (b): `grep 'OR-alternative framing'` (index.ts:12229) — same reasoning.
- visibility: Both consumers log when they fire (index.ts:11481 tap, index.ts:12229 console) but nothing logs when they DON'T — and the common case that suppresses them is silent: overrides (2) and (3) at index.ts:11021 and index.ts:11032 overwrite goalTargetDecision with `alternatives: []`, killing the reframe for every producer-lookup and shell-safety-net goal without a word. The lost deferral at index.ts:11489 produces no line at all; the resulting hollow note looks like a normal reach.
- **what must happen:** Pass the live `terminalOutputShapes` (and preferPathway/preferPathwayOrigin) into the alternative-framing retry at index.ts:11482-11496 instead of `undefined` — a reframe changes WHICH shapes are targeted, never whether the emit terminal must be content-bound. Make consumer (a)'s comparison set-based (sorted join) to match the producer's dedupe, so a permutation of the current post-override set is not mistaken for a new framing. And have consumer (b) at index.ts:12231 update goalTargetDecision.shapes when it reassigns seededOutputShapes, so index.ts:12237 escalates with the confidence of the set actually in use.

### `R1-infer-08` — agreement `FAIL` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `NEGATIVE` · load-bearing `high`

- agreement: CONFIRMED LIVE DEFECT — escalated from 'suspected' by RUNNING the module locally (bun test, no network, no credentials). With `complete` returning null (the LLM-declines case), the goal 'count the source code files and write the number to a memory note' returned {shapes:["source_code","memoryNote_write"], confidence:0.8} with decisionCache size 0; 'identify capabilities gated behind environment variables' returned {shapes:["env_gate_scan"], confidence:0.7} with decisionCache size 0. The mechanism is exactly as enumerated: remember() is called at goal-target-inference.ts:436, :482, :504, :523, :538, :558 and :722, but the `empty` value built at goal-target-inference.ts:334 — which is NOT empty, it carries a 0.4-0.8 deterministic decision — is returned RAW at :336, :337, :615, :624, :633 and :724. Both readers coalesce absence to null: index.ts:5448 (`inferredTargetDecisionCache.get(goalHashOf(goalText))?.confidence ?? null` in the recordGoalPath POST body) and index.ts:14633 (record.inferenceConfidence). Value-form hazard on top: `null` is indistinguishable from 'inference ran and was 0-confident', which is itself a real emitted value. Key form agrees on both sides (8-hex FNV goalHashOf). The fix comment at goal-target-inference.ts:339-355 asserts 'routing every exit through remember() makes this correct by construction' — six surviving raw exits falsify that claim, so the fictional column was narrowed, not closed, exactly where the deterministic fallbacks matter most: the LLM outage they exist to survive.
- **probe:** Two probes. (1) Already settled locally without a stack — `bun test` a harness that calls inferGoalTargetDecision('count the source code files and write the number to a memory note', [shellResult, memoryNote_write, concept_write, substrateGap_write, env_gate_scan, source_code], {complete: async () => null, decisionCache: new Map()}); HEALTHY = cache.size === 1 and cache value confidence === 0.8. Today it is 0. (2) Store side, against a locally booted stack: run `SELECT count() FROM goal_execution_paths WHERE last_inference_confidence = NONE GROUP ALL;` and `SELECT count() FROM goal_execution_paths GROUP ALL;` against the local container's SurrealDB, after driving ~50 mixed dispatches. HEALTHY = the NONE count is 0 (recordGoalPath sends a number for every dispatch that made a decision). Any non-zero NONE fraction after the fix lands is residual pre-fix rows; split by created_at to separate them.
- visibility: LOUD NEGATIVE, AND IT IS A PASSING TEST. test/goal-target-inference.test.ts:355-382 is a describe block titled 'shortcut decisions populate the decision cache' whose first case is annotated 'THE REGRESSION: this used to be 0'. It passes. It passes because its goals ('how many substrate gaps are there', 'count the activity templates') land on the SUBSTRATE-DATA AGGREGATE shortcut at goal-target-inference.ts:557, one of the seven that DO call remember(); the four deterministic decisions carried in the `empty` value are never exercised. A green regression suite is standing over a half-closed class and reading as proof the class is closed. Independently, `null` is a legal value in the option<number> column, so nothing downstream can distinguish 'never recorded' from 'recorded as unknown'.
- **what must happen:** Route the `empty` value through remember() too — i.e. compute it as `const empty = remember(deterministicCompositionAsk(...) ?? ...)` after the cache handle is hoisted, or wrap all six raw `return empty` statements. The class fix is a detector, not six edits: assert in the suite that EVERY return path of inferGoalTargetDecision that yields confidence > 0 leaves decisionCache.size === 1, driven by a table of goals covering all four deterministic routes AND the LLM-down / !r.ok / no-JSON / throw exits — because the existing test proves those exits are exactly what the current suite does not reach. Until then, no analysis correlating inference_confidence with reach is measuring what it claims.

### `R1-infer-09` — agreement `FAIL` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `NEGATIVE` · load-bearing `critical`

- agreement: CONFIRMED LIVE BYPASS — escalated from 'suspected' by RUNNING it. Producer verified: isCompositionAsk is computed at goal-target-inference.ts:395-396 from WRITE_CLAUSE, and I byte-diffed it against the hand-duplicated _COMPOSITION_WRITE_CLAUSE at goal-target-inference.ts:222 — IDENTICAL today (diff exit 0). Four readers confirmed, all as `!isCompositionAsk &&`: goal-target-inference.ts:503, :522, :537, :557. Two routes sitting ABOVE them return early without ever consulting it: the explicitly-named-shape route at goal-target-inference.ts:419-441 (added 2026-08-05) and the prose route at goal-target-inference.ts:459-483 (added 2026-07-26). The prose route is only partially covered by NOT_PROSE_RE at goal-target-inference.ts:478, which carries a THIRD independently-worded persist alternation — it catches 'write a note' but not 'record it in a memory note', since `record` is absent from it. Three textually separate copies of one intent, with nothing comparing them.
- **probe:** Settled locally without a stack: `bun test` a harness calling inferGoalTargetDecision('Run the test suite for repos/goal-host-vessel and record the pass count in a memory note', ['shellResult','test_suite','memoryNote_write'], {complete: async () => { throw new Error('LLM must not be reached'); }}). HEALTHY = the call THROWS (the guard stood the shortcut down and handed the composition to the LLM) or returns shapes containing 'memoryNote_write'. Today it returns ["test_suite"] and never throws. End-to-end confirmation on a local stack: dispatch that same goal to localhost:18210/run-goal, wait for terminal, then resolve the memoryNote store — HEALTHY = exactly 1 note recording the pass count exists AND reached == true; the predicted failure is reached == true with 0 notes, which is the hollow-green this seam produces.
- visibility: LOUD NEGATIVE, AND AGAIN IT IS A PASSING TEST. test/goal-target-inference.test.ts:233-255 is the guard's own regression suite ('does NOT truncate to a single compute shape', five COMPOSITIONS goals, asserted with a throwing LLM stub). It passes — but its vocabulary constant KC is ['shellResult','memoryNote_write','concept_write','source_code','substrateGap_write'], which contains no shape whose derived English phrase a COMPOSITIONS goal names after an action verb. So no test goal ever reaches the _namedShape route above the guard, and the suite is structurally incapable of observing the bypass. Worse, the reach gate greens: the plan is the truncated set, the walk produces exactly it, and plan-vs-observed reconciliation agrees because both sides are downstream of the same truncated plan (stated at goal-target-inference.ts:384-385). Nothing anywhere reports that a write clause was dropped.
- **what must happen:** Move the isCompositionAsk computation ABOVE the _namedShape block (it already sits above at goal-target-inference.ts:396 — the two routes simply do not read it) and guard BOTH: `if (!isCompositionAsk && _namedShape)` at goal-target-inference.ts:435 and add `!isCompositionAsk` to the prose predicate at goal-target-inference.ts:481, retiring NOT_PROSE_RE's redundant persist alternation so one detector governs. Collapse the three regex copies (:222, :395, the persist terms in :478) to one exported constant, and add the missing class detector: extend the COMPOSITIONS suite's vocabulary to include an action-verb-nameable shape such as test_suite, so a shortcut added ABOVE the guard fails the test instead of shipping. The generator question (law 6): the system should have minted this goal from its own observation that a reached dispatch produced no note — that missing generator is a gap in its own right.

### `R1-infer-10` — agreement `PASS` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `NONE` · load-bearing `medium`

- agreement: Seam exists; key forms and value forms agree between the (b) writer and the (b) reader. TWO CORRECTIONS TO THE ENUMERATION. (1) The claimed cross-namespace eviction hazard ('an (a) write can evict a (b) entry and vice versa') is NOT REACHABLE IN PRODUCTION, because namespace (a) is never written: its only writer is inferGoalTargetShapes (goal-target-inference.ts:195-201), and `grep -rn` over repos/goal-host-vessel/src shows the symbol appears exactly once outside its own module — the import at index.ts:246 — with no invocation. It is kept green by 19 references in test/. So the Map is single-namespace at runtime and the `cache: inferredTargetShapeCache` argument at index.ts:10957 is an INERT argument: inferGoalTargetDecision reads only opts.decisionCache, never opts.cache (verified by reading the whole function, goal-target-inference.ts:329-726). (2) The set-difference reconstruction at goal-target-inference.ts:798-801 is sound as the inventory says — the key embeds the sorted target set — but the real consequence stands: the LLM's `intermediate` ORDER is discarded on a cache HIT and rebuilt as `inferredTargets.filter(...)`, i.e. in the caller's order, while index.ts:11112 depends on that order to sequence production. So a warm dispatch and a cold dispatch for the same goal can sequence intermediates differently. That is a behavioural divergence, not a name/type disagreement, hence PASS on agreement.
- **probe:** On a local stack, dispatch the SAME multi-shape derivation goal twice within the 512-entry cache window: `curl -s localhost:18210/run-goal -d '{"goal":"probe-nonce-E fetch repos/activity-api/package.json, count its dependencies, and write the number to a memory note"}'` twice. Then `docker exec substrate-live journalctl -u goal-host-vessel | grep 'derivation-intent intermediates'` (the tap at index.ts:11114-11117 emits intermediate_shapes as JSON). HEALTHY = the two lines carry IDENTICAL intermediate_shapes arrays including ORDER — count of order-mismatched pairs = 0. A mismatch is the cold-vs-warm divergence. Separately, to confirm namespace (a) is dead at runtime rather than merely uncalled in source: after driving 50 dispatches, no key in inferredTargetShapeCache should lack the `split:` prefix — expose that count via a debug shape or a one-line tap; HEALTHY = 100% of keys prefixed `split:`.
- visibility: Nothing distinguishes a split cache HIT from a MISS — the tap at index.ts:11114 fires identically either way, so the order loss on a warm hit is invisible at the only place anyone looks. Nothing reports that the `cache:` argument at index.ts:10957 is never read; it typechecks, so no gate observes it. The dead namespace is likewise invisible: inferGoalTargetShapes has full test coverage, so the suite is green and the function looks live.
- **what must happen:** Cache the whole DerivationSplit ({intermediate, terminal}) rather than the terminal list alone, so a warm hit reproduces the cold ordering exactly; and give it its own Map instead of sharing one with a namespace nothing writes. Then remove the inert `cache:` argument at index.ts:10957 and either delete inferGoalTargetShapes with its tests or annotate it as dead — a fully-tested function with no production call site is the 'script nothing invokes' pattern the repo conventions name: it cannot be observed failing, so it can never be trusted when it passes.

### `R1-override-11` — agreement `FAIL` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `WEAK` · load-bearing `high`

- agreement: CONFIRMED plane split: after this point the SHAPES plane and the CONFIDENCE plane describe different decisions. All four overrides verified in place — countable UNION at index.ts:10986-10995 (touches seededOutputShapes only, leaves goalTargetDecision alone); SHELL SAFETY NET at index.ts:11005-11025 (sets both, confidence 0.5, alternatives blanked); PRODUCER-LOOKUP OVERRIDE at index.ts:11030-11035, unconditional for its pattern by design (comment at index.ts:11026-11029), confidence 0.7, alternatives blanked; COMPUTE-CHAIN AUGMENTATION at index.ts:11043-11058, rebuilding goalTargetDecision while carrying the OLD confidence forward, with a `.slice(0, 3)` at index.ts:11054 that can drop the write terminal it just re-ordered when _rest is non-empty. None of the four writes back to inferredTargetDecisionCache, which is the ONLY thing index.ts:5448 and index.ts:14633 read. So `inference_confidence` persisted to goal_execution_paths and surfaced on the dispatch record describes a decision that overrides (2), (3) and (4) may have replaced — any correlation of confidence against reach is measuring a superseded decision. ONE SCOPE CORRECTION IN THE INVENTORY'S FAVOUR, VERIFIED: all four sit inside the `if (!seededOutputShapes || seededOutputShapes.length === 0)` block opened at index.ts:10676 and closed just before the derivation-split block at index.ts:11060, so they never touch a dispatch whose caller declared expected_output_shapes. Secondary confirmed consequence: overrides (2) and (3) blank alternatives to [], killing the reframe retry at index.ts:11472 for every producer-lookup and shell-safety-net goal.
- **probe:** On a local stack, dispatch a producer-lookup goal that inference would otherwise answer well: `curl -s localhost:18210/run-goal -d '{"goal":"probe-nonce-F which vessel serves the shape memoryNote_write"}'`. Then grep the goal-host journal for the two lines that MUST both appear for this dispatch: `goal-target inference` (index.ts:10961, carrying the pre-override confidence) and `producer-lookup override seeded shellResult` (index.ts:11034). Read the dispatch record: `curl -s localhost:18210/executions/<dispatchId>` and read inferenceConfidence (written at index.ts:14633). HEALTHY = inferenceConfidence == 0.7, matching the override that actually decided the target. Today's predicted value is whatever the pre-override inference cached, or null. Fleet reading: over 100 dispatches, count rows where an override line appears in the journal AND the recorded inference_confidence differs from the override's constant — HEALTHY = 0.

But the confidence plane is not: nothing logs that the recorded confidence no longer describes the decision in force, and nothing reads those taps. The recorded value is a well-formed number in a legal column, which is why this survives; it is the same 'plausible-looking value' class as infer-08, one layer further down.

### `R1-store-14` — agreement `PASS` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `WEAK` · load-bearing `high`

- agreement: Agreement holds CONDITIONALLY ON TODAY'S CALLER, and the seam is correctly described.
