TWO HASH IDENTITIES CONFIRMED AND THEY ARE NEVER COMPARED ACROSS THE BOUNDARY: goalHashOf (goal-target-inference.ts:39-45) is NFC→lowercase→collapse→trim→FNV-1a→8 lowercase hex; hashGoal (goal-paths.ts:175-178) is normalizeGoal (lowercase→trim→elide digit-bearing hex runs of >=8 to 'id'→elide 10+ digit runs to 'n'→strip punctuation→spaces to underscores) then md5 truncated to 16 hex. Because the store re-derives, no datum crosses in the wrong space — so this is a LEGIBILITY defect, not a data defect, and index.ts:5433 is where it bites: one log line prints `goal_hash=<FNV-8> borrowed_from_goal=<md5-16>`, reading as one identifier space when it is two. LATENT NULL-vs-NONE CONFIRMED AND CORRECTLY SCOPED: goal-paths.ts:686 binds `validated.expected_output_shapes ?? null` into `expected_output_shapes` on the CREATE path, where the column is `option<array<string>>` (sql/migrations/188-goal-paths-expected-shapes.surql:26), and the schema marks the request field optional (src/models/schemas.ts:796) — so a caller omitting it binds JS null into an option<T>, the exact class the sibling line at goal-paths.ts:700-703 explicitly defends against for inference_confidence with `?? 0` and a comment stating 'passing NULL fails the whole CREATE silently'. Today's caller never triggers it: recordGoalPath defaults `expectedOutputShapes: string[] = []` at index.ts:5427 and all seven call sites (index.ts:10340, :10375, :11367, :11374, :11592, :11607, :12343) pass an array. The bind is latently lossy for any other caller, and the CREATE would return an empty result set while the caller logged success.
- **probe:** On a local stack, drive 20 dispatches then query the local container's SurrealDB: `SELECT count() FROM goal_execution_paths GROUP ALL;` before and after. HEALTHY = the count increases by the number of dispatches that produced a non-empty path_activities (recordGoalPath returns early on an empty path, index.ts:5428). Then, to separate 'never sent' from 'sent empty': `SELECT count() FROM goal_execution_paths WHERE expected_output_shapes = NONE GROUP ALL;` versus `SELECT count() FROM goal_execution_paths WHERE expected_output_shapes = [] GROUP ALL;` — HEALTHY = the NONE count is 0 for rows created after the probe run, since every current caller sends an array. A non-zero NONE count among fresh rows is the latent bind having become live. Also assert the producer leg is not silently rejected: `docker exec substrate-live journalctl -u goal-host-vessel | grep -c 'goal-path record REJECTED\|goal-path record FAILED'` — HEALTHY = 0.
- visibility: Better than most of this stretch and deliberately so: a non-2xx warns at index.ts:5463-5465 and a throw warns at index.ts:5466-5468, both naming that the walk will not inform future reuse — the comment records that this was previously a silent `catch {}` producing an 8x optimism bias. But it is still log-only with no counter at the consuming side, and the consumer's own success path logs `logger.info('Updated goal path')` at goal-paths.ts:632-640, which reports on the post-UPDATE state and would look identical if a bound field had silently not been written. The latent CREATE-null case would produce NO error at all on either side — the SurrealDB CREATE returns an empty result set and the caller logs success — which is why the sibling defence at goal-paths.ts:700-703 exists.
- **what must happen:** Apply the defence that already exists one line below: change goal-paths.ts:686 to bind `validated.expected_output_shapes ?? undefined` (or omit the field) so an absent array cannot silently abort the CREATE — and make it a class fix by auditing every CONTENT bind in this file against its migration's option<T> columns, since the comment at goal-paths.ts:700-703 proves the class was found once and patched at one site. Separately, stop printing the two hash spaces as one line at index.ts:5433: label them (`local_goal_hash=` / `store_goal_hash=`) or drop the FNV one, because a reader correlating a journal hash against a store row today is silently comparing incomparable identifiers.

## LEARNING 2 — minting structure from observation


All 16 enumerated seams exist as described; both sides opened and every file:line anchor re-verified against the working tree. AGREEMENT settled statically for 15 of 16 (one sub-claim on SurrealDB record-vs-string equality left to the probe, but seam 07 FAILs under any coercion on the prefix form alone). Headline: the entire earned-structure spine is statically dead. (a) L2-structure-04's CREATE cannot satisfy the SCHEMAFULL table — `execution_id` and `success` are ASSERT-!=-NONE with no DEFAULT and are never bound — so no execution has ever minted an edge; only the UPDATE branch can fire, and only onto rows the masked batch reconciler already wrote. (b) L2-structure-07's readback compares a bare-slug string column to `$parent.id`, which the code's own prefix-strip at discover-by-shapes.ts:241 proves renders as `activity:⟨slug⟩` — so `composition_score` is null on every candidate on both branches, and `predecessor_activity_id` is never sent (0 hits in goal-host). The composition-learning limb is therefore write-dead AND read-dead simultaneously. (c) NEW, not in the inventory: L2-structure-09's `ExtractLifecycle` (ribosome-vessel/src/index.ts:192-206) has no `goalSignature` key; engine.ts:280 lifts `variables.lifecycle` into `lifecycleContext`, and resolveLifecyclePlaceholders THROWS UNRESOLVABLE_PLACEHOLDER on a not-found path — so every ribosome-vessel-dispatched extraction dies at `synthesize_template`, which is a hard mint failure, not the "null back-edge" the inventory suspected. (d) NEW: `anchor:'{{anchor}}'` in ribosome-extract.json:177 is resolved by neither of the engine's two config passes ({{lifecycle.*}}, {{impulse:*}}), so the literal string `{{anchor}}` reaches pointer.anchor — and nothing on the consuming side reads pointer.anchor at all, making it written-never-read and the template's own "required by the write resolver contract" note stale. Visibility across the stretch is the dominant pathology: not one of these 16 hops increments a counter a consumer reads; four are NEGATIVE (04, 12, 13, 14) in the strict sense that a healthy-looking signal is emitted while the seam is dead. Enumeration corrections are in each agreement_detail: seam 11's "loses extracted_from" is an overstatement (`??=` never overwrites), seam 14's failure_mode is `reuse_existing_producer` (the "refused duplicate mint" text is in `detail`), seam 16 has an additional silent `limit:100` truncation the inventory missed, and seam 04's UPDATE branch is a real nuance the inventory collapsed. No claim in this report uses production access; the inventory's row counts, mtimes, mask states and journal greps are cited as inventory-reported and unverified here. Incidental find while reading engine.ts: a stray `console.error("[rIS-debug]", …)` at repos/ias-executor-ts/src/engine.ts:295 inside resolveImpulseSlot — an uncommitted debug artifact on a hot path.

### `L2-structure-04` — agreement `FAIL` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `NEGATIVE` · load-bearing `critical`

- agreement: CONFIRMED STRUCTURALLY DEAD ON THE CREATE BRANCH, statically, by two INDEPENDENT kills. (1) Unbound required columns: the CREATE at execution-traces.ts:1637-1644 sets only parent_activity_id, child_activity_id, execution_count, success_count, weight, created_at, updated_at. The SCHEMAFULL definition requires `execution_id` TYPE string ASSERT $value != NONE with NO DEFAULT (011-executions.surql:289-291) and `success` TYPE bool ASSERT $value != NONE with NO DEFAULT (:305-307) — neither is bound; `success` appears only inside `IF($success, …)` counter arithmetic, never as a column. `org_id` (:293-296) has `VALUE $value OR <string>$auth.org_id`, which saves it only under a JWT. I checked `account_id_version` separately: migrations/095-account-id-additive.surql:106 gives it `DEFAULT 0`, so unlike the reconciler's CONTENT upsert it is NOT a third kill on this CREATE path. (2) Table PERMISSIONS `FOR create WHERE $auth.org_id != NONE` (011-executions.surql:275-279 and migrations/055:13-24) — on the no-JWT branch at :1650 (`surrealDB.query` on root, $auth empty) create is denied outright. NUANCE THE INVENTORY COLLAPSED: the UPDATE branch (:1631-1636) binds nothing new and CAN legitimately fire — the reconciler stores `parent_activity_id`/`child_activity_id` as the same bare trace `activity_id` slugs this helper computes, so a nested execution over an already-reconciled pair silently increments execution_count/success_count on a batch-written row. The seam is therefore not inert: it can corrupt batch counts with exit-status 'success' while being unable to mint a single new edge.

Then, from inside the container: `SELECT count() FROM activity_composition_graph WHERE execution_id != 'composition-edge-reconcile'` — healthy >=1, current static prediction 0. DISCRIMINATING second probe (separates 'invoked and asserting' from 'never invoked'): `docker exec substrate-live journalctl -u activity-api --since T | grep -c 'derive-from-parent failed'` — healthy 0 AND the count above >=1. A count of 0 in BOTH means the limb never ran (seam 01 dead); >0 in the log with 0 rows confirms the assert kill.
- visibility: LOUD FLAG. Three compounding concealments. (i) The assert/permission throw is caught at :1652 and logged only as a warn, and the caller's `.catch(() => {})` at :2367 discards even that path's rejection. (ii) The table is NOT empty — it holds the batch reconciler's rows — so every reader (row counts, the composition graph endpoint, dashboards) sees a populated composition graph and reports health while the execution-earned writer has never landed a single row. A non-zero row count is the exact signal that concealed this for weeks. (iii) The UPDATE branch succeeding on reconciler rows produces genuine write activity that is indistinguishable from the seam working.
- **what must happen:** Bind `execution_id` (the child trace's execution_id) and `success` in the CREATE, thread org_id from `traceOrgId`, and stop falling back to the root connection where create permission is denied. Then add a counter at the consumer distinguishing edges CREATEd vs UPDATEd vs skipped, and alert on 'zero created in 24h while nested traces > 0' — that is the number that would have exposed this.

Note T = now. `docker exec substrate-live systemctl start composition-edge-reconcile.service`, wait for exit, then `SELECT count() FROM activity_composition_graph WHERE execution_id = 'composition-edge-reconcile' AND updated_at > <T>` — healthy: equals the `wrote` count on stderr (`docker exec substrate-live journalctl -u composition-edge-reconcile --since T | grep -E 'mode=|upsert-failed|skip-malformed-edge'`). Healthy: 0 lines matching `upsert-failed` and `skip-malformed-edge`; a nonzero `wrote`. Second probe for the ordering seam: run twice and check `mode=incremental` on the second run — `mode=full` twice means last_full_at never advanced.
- visibility: Log-only, and only on stderr: `mode=…` at :171, `skip-malformed-edge` at :465, `upsert-failed` at :492. Nothing counts rows written into a store any consumer reads, and the per-edge catch means a run that fails EVERY edge still exits successfully. A systemd timer that runs and writes nothing looks exactly like one that runs and writes everything.
- **what must happen:** Move the `last_full_at` write before the write loop (or make it a separate guaranteed step) so one abort cannot pin full mode forever; emit `wrote`/`skipped`/`failed` as a structured line and, better, as a row a consumer reads, so 'the reconcile ran but landed nothing' is detectable without reading stderr.

### `L2-structure-07` — agreement `FAIL` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `NEGATIVE` · load-bearing `critical`

- agreement: UPGRADED FROM THE INVENTORY'S 'SUSPECT' TO A CONFIRMED STATIC FAIL, and on a ground that does not depend on SurrealDB's record-vs-string equality semantics. The correlated subquery at repos/activity-api/src/services/discover-by-shapes.ts:168-176 compares `child_activity_id` — TYPE string (011-executions.surql:285), populated by the reconciler with the BARE trace `activity_id` slug (execution-traces.ts:1885 = `body.activity_id || body.template_id`) — against `$parent.id`, the id of a row selected `FROM activity` (query at :230-236). That id renders as `activity:<slug>` (with ⟨⟩ where quoting requires it), which the file itself proves: :241-243 and :266 both strip `/^activity:/` and `[⟨⟩`]` off `String(row.id)` before using it as a map key. So even under full string coercion the two operands differ by the `activity:` prefix and can never match; if SurrealDB additionally refuses record==string, that is a second, independent kill. Consequence traced to the end: `comp_row` is null → :307 `_comp_row: comp_row ?? null` → :330-338 `composition_score` is gated on `(compRow.execution_count || 0) > 0` and therefore null on EVERY candidate. I own the second half of the enumeration's claim myself: `grep -c predecessor_activity_id repos/goal-host-vessel/src/index.ts` returns 0, so only the child-global `GROUP ALL` rollup branch is ever taken in production — a branch that in any case cannot distinguish A→B from C→B.

Pick any `child_activity_id` value present in activity_composition_graph, take the shape it produces, then: `docker exec substrate-live curl -s -X POST localhost:8080/v2/activities/discover-by-shapes -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"mode":"candidates_with_scores","required_shapes":["<that shape>"]}' | jq '[.activities[]|select(.composition_score != null)]|length'`. Healthy: >=1. Static prediction: 0, on every shape. Confirmatory variant that isolates the prefix: run the same query after a one-off `SELECT * FROM activity_composition_graph WHERE child_activity_id = type::string($some_activity_record_id)` vs `= 'bare-slug'` — whichever returns rows names the correct form.
- visibility: LOUD FLAG. `composition_score: null` is a perfectly legitimate value meaning 'this pair has no history yet', so a permanently-null field is indistinguishable from a young graph. Downstream, goal-host's blendEdgeScore simply contributes nothing and the pick still returns a ranked list — the walk looks healthy and merely never benefits from composition history. Nothing counts the null rate, so a 100%-null population raises no signal anywhere.
- **what must happen:** Normalize both sides to one form at the comparison (`child_activity_id = record::id($parent.id)` or store the wrapped form), and add a counter at the consumer for 'candidates returned with non-null composition_score / candidates returned' — an all-null ratio sustained over a window is the number that would have caught this. Until the write side (seam 04) also lands, fixing the read alone still yields only batch-derived scores.

It is then INERT: `anchor` sits on the pointer, not on templateData, and the consumer at impulses.ts:2679-2743 delegates only `writePointer.templateData` to /templates and never reads `pointer.anchor` — so the field is written-never-read, and the template's own note (:190) claiming 'the anchor field is required by the write resolver contract to prevent anchor_not_found errors' is stale documentation, not a live requirement. Second, the datum-form mismatch the inventory named is real and the consumer has accreted two compensating repairs that are direct evidence of degradation: fence-strip + balanced-brace slice for a string body (impulses.ts:2690-2715, returning 400 'templateData is an unparseable string' when both fail) and a deterministic `description` default (:2720-2727) whose own comment says LLM proposals 'recurrently omit description despite the prompt stating the contract'. Third, determinism of the `learned-<parent-slug>` id is enforced ONLY by prompt text (:119 derivation rule 1), never by code — confirmed.

Force one reach→mint (seam 08 probe), then inspect the extract run's tasks: `docker exec substrate-live curl -s 'localhost:8080/v2/activities/execution-traces?limit=20' | jq '[.traces[]|select(.template_id=="ribosome-extract")]|.[0].tasks[]|{task_id,success,error}'`. Healthy: synthesize_template success=true AND dispatch_write_attempt success=true. Then confirm the parse path was not exercised: `journalctl -u activity-api | grep -c 'templateData is an unparseable string'` — healthy 0 over a window with >0 mints; any nonzero is the LLM-format seam degrading.

But the caller is a best-effort http task inside a chain whose notes explicitly say 'when the resolver fails … the chain still completes - extract is best-effort' (ribosome-extract.json:190) — so a 400 here is absorbed and the run still reports completed. Nobody counts how often the fence-parse fallback or the description default fires, which are precisely the two counters that would quantify this seam's degradation rate.

Instrument the two compensating repairs with counters — 'templateData arrived as string', 'description defaulted' — since each firing is a measurement of how far the LLM output drifts from the contract, and today that rate is unknown.

The consumer's call is unawaited (`onTemplateCreated(msg, replayDeps)` with no await, index.ts:631) so even a thrown error inside it is invisible to the caller. Consumer resolveSubstrateGapWrite at :315: all four gates confirmed — missing `gap` (:317-333), missing/empty `gap.id` with the explicit 'the field is `id`, not `gap_id`' guidance (:352-365), empty-summary-or-`{{`-in-id/category on an OPEN gap (:369-386), and the timestamp-placeholder SCRUB at :388-402 that silently rewrites unbound `{{…}}` timestamps to the server clock. Value-form note confirmed: `coerceFlatGapPointer` (:316-317) accepts a FLAT pointer carrying summary/detail/description/title and rewrites it into `pointer.gap` — a shape-repair layer whose existence is itself evidence that callers disagree with the declared contract. The inventory's live evidence (29,998 bytes, mtime Aug 4, dev-vessel masked) is INVENTORY-REPORTED AND UNVERIFIED HERE — I have no production access.
- **probe:** Local stack, development-vessel running. Note T. Trigger any detector (or POST a gap directly): `docker exec substrate-live curl -s -X POST localhost:8090/v2/impulses/resolve -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"impulse":{"type":"substrateGap_write","gap":{"id":"probe-<nonce>","category":"other","summary":"liveness probe","status":"open"}}}'`. Then read the store at the CONSUMING side: `docker exec substrate-live stat -c '%s %y' /workspace/gaps/gaps.json` — healthy: mtime > T. And `docker exec substrate-live jq '[.[]|select(.status=="open")]|length' /workspace/gaps/gaps.json` — healthy: rises by 1. A file whose mtime never advances while detectors run is the frozen-store signature.
- visibility: Each of the four gates returns a legibly-worded structuredError to the caller — genuinely good consumer-facing feedback, better than most of this stretch. But nothing aggregates it: no counter of gaps-written vs gaps-rejected-per-gate, and the SCRUB at :388-402 is by design SILENT (it rewrites a bad value rather than reporting it). Above all, the store's own staleness has no watchdog — a gaps.json that stops advancing produces no signal, and the step-0 problem is real: an outage in the gap-filing path structurally prevents filing a gap about itself.
- **what must happen:** Add an external freshness check on the store (age of newest detected_at vs detector run cadence) that does NOT itself route through substrateGap_write, so the detect→record loop can report its own failure. Count writes and per-gate rejections. Log the timestamp SCRUB rather than performing it silently, since each firing is an unbound-slot defect in a caller.

### `L2-structure-16` — agreement `FAIL` · liveness `UNOBSERVABLE-PENDING-LOCAL-STACK` · visibility `NONE` · load-bearing `critical`

- agreement: CONFIRMED — the cleanest value-domain agreement failure in the stretch, fully static. Consumer repos/boredom-vessel/src/goal-generation.ts:245-247: `const ACTIONABLE_CATEGORIES = new Set(['ui_legibility']); const categoryActionable = ACTIONABLE_CATEGORIES.has(String(g.category ?? '')); if (!categoryActionable && !/capability|repair/i.test(g.summary)) continue;`. Producer domain (my own enumeration over development-vessel resolvers/seed): ~30 distinct category literals including systematic_failure, architectural_pattern, missing_capability, missing_concept, orphaned_capability, activity_lifecycle, detector_coverage_gap, vessel_exercise_zero_coverage, remedy_livelock, posterior_consistency_drift, operational_health, trace_quality, learning_loop, documentation_drift. INTERSECTION WITH THE ADMIT SET: exactly ONE (ui_legibility). Both sides use the key `category`, both use strings, both agree on type — and the value domains are disjoint on 29 of ~30 members. Everything else is admitted only by the accident of its free-prose summary containing 'capability' or 'repair'; the file's own comment (:222-224) records the measured cost of the prose-only predicate: 'it drops 247 of 524 open gaps (47%), including 100% of ui_legibility'. ENUMERATION ADDITION the inventory missed: there is a SECOND silent truncation UPSTREAM of the category gate — the source request at :107-109 is `{type:'substrateGap', status:'open', limit:100, sort:'disposition_scored'}`, i.e. at most 100 of the (comment-reported) 524 open gaps are even fetched before any admission logic runs. Also confirmed: `admitActionableGapsMirror` at :82-95 (a self-described MIRROR of a dev-vessel gate, which by construction can drift from the thing it mirrors) and the CATEGORY_WEIGHT sort at :127 whose weight table names nine categories, six of which the gate at :247 will then drop anyway — the ranking and the admission disagree with each other.
- **probe:** Local stack, dev-vessel + boredom-vessel running, with a populated gaps.json. Measure the drop rate at the consumer: `docker exec substrate-live curl -s -X POST localhost:8090/v2/impulses/resolve -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"impulse":{"type":"substrateGap","status":"open","limit":100,"sort":"disposition_scored"}}' | jq '[.body.gaps[]]|length as $in | [.[]|select(.category=="ui_legibility" or (.summary|test("capability|repair";"i")))]|length as $out | {$in,$out}'` — healthy: out/in > 0.8. Then the true end-to-end number: `docker exec substrate-live journalctl -u boredom-vessel --since T | grep -c 'reserving gap-goal:'` against the same window's fetch count. Healthy: gap-goals reserved > 0 whenever open actionable gaps > 0.
- visibility: The drop is a bare `continue` at :247 with no counter, no log, no aggregate — 47% of the gap store falls on the floor and NOTHING anywhere reports the rate. Note the asymmetry, which is itself the tell: the ADMIT path logs ('[gap-goal-supply] ADMITTED by category …', :249) while the DROP path is silent, so the journal shows only successes. There is one good precedent in the same file to copy — the supply-failure warn at :112-117, added precisely because 'returning [] silently is indistinguishable in the journal from candidates existed but scored low'; the same reasoning applies verbatim here and was not applied.
- **what must happen:** Count the drop: emit admitted/dropped-by-prose-gate/dropped-by-mirror/truncated-by-limit per pass, so the 47% is a number somebody reads. Then fix the agreement properly — replace the one-element ACTIONABLE_CATEGORIES plus prose fallback with an explicit mapping over the producer's actual ~30-value category domain (or a shared, single-source-of-truth actionability field written by the detector), and reconcile it with the CATEGORY_WEIGHT table that currently ranks categories the gate discards.
